## Q2086. Remove lacunarity from pairwise-independent means

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.3) · **Collection** 21

For pairwise independent identically distributed X_n with E X_n=0 and E|X_n|≤μ<∞, do Y_n=(X₁+⋯+X_n)/n have an explicit uniform learnable bound depending only on μ,λ,ε, without restrictions on b_k/a_k?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2085, 2086: Remark 5.2 and the first question preceding Problem 5.3. Setup for questions 2085, 2086: For real random variables Y_n, put Cε(a,b)={max_{a≤i,j≤b}|Y_i−Y_j|>ε}. A uniform learnable bound ψ satisfies: for every a₀&lt;b₀≤a₁&lt;b₁≤⋯ and λ,ε∈(0,1], some k≤ψ(λ,ε) has P(Cε(a_k,b_k))≤λ. Let Jε be the supremum of k with i₁&lt;j₁≤⋯≤i_k&lt;j_k and |Y_{i_l}−Y_{j_l}|>ε for every l.

**Source.** Morenikeji Neri. *Oscillations in the Strong Law of Large Numbers, 2026 preprint; Proceedings AMS forthcoming*. 2026. [primary source](https://kejineri.github.io/Papers/Oscillations_in_the_SLLN_submitted.pdf) Location: §5, p. 19, first question preceding Problem 5.3; Theorem 1.5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2085, 2086, checked 6 October 2026: current author manuscript retains these questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01676) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22741) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08078) · [4](https://nicholaspischke.github.io/bib/ref_name.html)


<a id="q2087"></a>

## Q2087. Small reinforcement in dimensions three to five

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

For every d∈{3,4,5}, is there a_d>0 such that this walk on Z^d is almost surely transient whenever 0&lt;a&lt;a_d?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2087, 2088, 2089: Lattice phase conjecture: Vladas Sidoravicius, circa 2010; first printed in Beffara’s 2011 habilitation. Setup for questions 2087, 2088, 2089: A walk starts at 0. Each undirected edge initially has weight 1, permanently changed to 1+a after its first crossing, a>0. At every discrete step it selects an incident edge proportionally to its current weight. Recurrence means infinitely many returns to 0.

**Source.** Dor Elboim and Gady Kozma. *Once-reinforced random walk in high dimensions, 2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17972) Location: Introduction and §1.1, pp. 1–2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2087, 2088, 2089, checked 6 October 2026: September range paper still records the lattice gap; no strip resolution found.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B5: submitted unresolved): No new proof, counterexample, or independently validated exact-scope resolution was obtained in this investigation. Remaining/limits: The exact retained statement remains unresolved in this investigation. Intake: Scope and identity checked; no independent proof or renewed literature audit of this question. [Report](../../solutions/b5-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00090) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.07167) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1214/21-AIHP1151)

## Q2090. Upper range bound for strong reinforcement

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

For every d≥2, does some β₀(d)<∞ satisfy Eβ|R_n|=O_{d,β}(n^{d/(d+1)}) for every fixed β≥β₀(d), as n→∞?

**Context.** Origin for question 2090: Ordemann–Berkolaiko–Havlin–Bunde (2000), site version; Foster–Grassberger–Paczuski (2009), edge version; Beffara (2011). Setup for question 2090: On Z^d, start at 0; untraversed edges have weight 1 and traversed edges weight β≥1. Choose the next edge proportionally to weight. Set R_n={X₀,…,X_n}.

**Source.** Ahmed Bou-Rabee and Yuval Peres. *Once-reinforced random walk on Z^d has range exponent at least d/(d+1), 2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00090) Location: Introduction, pp. 1–2, predicted order preceding Theorem 1.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2090, checked 6 October 2026: September paper proves only the matching lower bound.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B5: submitted unresolved): No new proof, counterexample, or independently validated exact-scope resolution was obtained in this investigation. <a id="q2091"></a>

## Q2091. Replacer suppression at every fitness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

For all N≥3 and r>0, must ρ\_R(G,r)<(1−1/r)/(1−r^(−N)), with the right side interpreted as 1/N at r=1?

**Context.** Origin for question 2091: Authors’ concluding question, discovered through Svoboda–Chatterjee doctoral genealogy. Setup for question 2091: On a finite connected simple undirected N-vertex graph, start one uniformly placed mutant of reproductive rate r>0 and residents of rate 1. Each step chooses a parent proportionally to rate; its offspring replaces a uniformly chosen opposite-type neighbor, or nothing changes if none exists. Let ρ\_R(G,r) be mutant fixation probability.

**Source.** Michal Pecho. *Jakub Svoboda, Lenka Kopfová, Josef Tkadlec and Krishnendu Chatterjee, Replacers and their evolutionary stability in the Moran process on graphs, 2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06552) Location: Discussion, pp. 9–10; Theorem 3, p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2091, checked 6 October 2026: source proves neutral and r≥2 cases; remaining strengths unresolved.


<a id="q2092"></a>

## Q2092. Vanishing minimal-measurement injectivity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 21

Is lim_{M→∞} p_M=0?

**Context.** Origin for question 2092: Cynthia Vinzant’s refined conjecture (2015); restated by Bandeira and coauthors (2026). Setup for question 2092: For M≥2, draw A∈C^{(4M−5)×M} with independent standard complex Gaussian entries. Let p_M be the probability that x↦|Ax| is injective modulo multiplication by a unit complex scalar.

**Source.** Zhangsong Li. *On Injectivity of Phase Retrieval, 2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17922) Location: Conjecture 1.1(2), pp. 1–2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2092, checked 6 October 2026: Li proves p_M<1, leaving the asymptotic part.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B7: submitted unresolved): No new analysis or status promotion in this return. Remaining/limits: not_attempted Intake: Scope and identity checked; no independent proof or renewed literature audit of this question. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29571)


<a id="q2093"></a>

## Q2093. Sharp mobility-response rate

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

As λ↓0, is |ℓ(λ)/λ−Σe₁|=O(λ) for d≥3 and O(λ√log(1/λ)) for d=2, with constants depending only on d,Λ,r₀?

**Context.** Origin for question 2093: Authors’ conjectured upper bound (1.11). Setup for question 2093: On R^d, d≥2, let (a,V) be jointly stationary with finite dependence range r₀. Assume a symmetric, Λ⁻¹I≤a≤ΛI, |V|≤Λ, and both fields Λ-Lipschitz. Set L=(1/2)e^{2V}div(e^{−2V}a∇), L_λ=L+λae₁·∇. Write ℓ(λ) for the deterministic limiting velocity under L_λ and Σ for the covariance matrix in the L-invariance principle.

**Source.** Ahmed Bou-Rabee and Ruizhe Xu. *Quantitative Einstein relation for reversible diffusions in a random environment, 2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26082) Location: Assumption 1.1; §1.4, equation (1.11), p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2093, checked 6 October 2026: source and current author page retain the sharper rate.

**Further links.** [1](https://nitromannitol.github.io/)


<a id="q2094"></a>

## Q2094. Low-dimensional block-beta faces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.2) · **Collection** 21

For β\_i≥0, must E f_j(P_n)≍a_n also hold for 0≤j<⌊d/2⌋−1?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2094, 2095, 2096: Authors’ §5. Setup for questions 2094, 2095, 2096: Let d=Σd_i≥2, d_i≥1. Let P_n be the hull of n iid points in ∏B^{d_i}; blocks are independent, block i having density proportional to (1−|x|²)^{β\_i}, β\_i>−1. Set k_i=(d_i+β\_i)/(1+β\_i), K=max k_i, s=#{i:k_i=K}, a_n=n^{(K−1)/(K+1)}(log n)^{s−1}; f_j counts j-faces.

**Source.** Florian Besau. *Anna Gusakova and Christoph Thäle, Random polytopes in convex bodies: Bridging the gap between extremal containers, 2024*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19163) Location: Conjecture 5.2, p. 34.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2094, 2095, 2096, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution located; β\_i≥0 facet orders are proved.

**Further links.** [1](https://sites.google.com/site/florianbesau/research)

## Q2097. Reconstructing the low-temperature plus phase

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.5) · **Collection** 21

Does zero-field nearest-neighbor Ising on Z_n² at fixed β>β\_c, conditioned on positive total magnetization, admit nondegenerate RSR?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2097, 2098, 2099, 2100: Authors’ Questions 8.5, 8.9 and 8.13. Setup for questions 2097, 2098, 2099, 2100: Put C(f,U)=Var(E[f|σ\_U])/Var(f). RSR means Boolean f_n and spin-independent random U_n with max_vP(v∈U_n)→0 and inf_n E C(f_n,U_n)>0; nondegenerate additionally requires inf_n Var(f_n)>0. For the tree questions, P is free zero-field Ising on the d-regular tree, d≥3, with (d−1)⁻¹&lt;tanhβ≤(d−1)⁻¹/².

**Source.** Pál Galicza and Gábor Pete. *Sparse reconstruction in spin systems II: Ising and other factor of IID measures, 2025; journal 2026*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09232) Location: Question 8.5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2097, 2098, 2099, 2100, checked 6 October 2026: journal retains these questions; no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00440-025-01395-4) · [2](https://d-nb.info/1374750980/34)

<a id="q2185"></a>

## Q2185. Semideciding limsup-average POMDP values

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 22

Given rational x∈[0,1] and rational ε>0 with the promise v>x+ε or v&lt;x−ε, is there a Turing machine that accepts every first-case input and never accepts a second-case input?

**Context.** Origin for question 2185: Chatterjee–Saona–Ziliotto, Finite-Memory Strategies in POMDPs with Long-Run Average Objectives (preprint 2019; MOR 2022). Setup for question 2185: A finite rational POMDP has finite hidden states, actions and signals, rational initial belief and transition/signal kernel, and rational rewards in [0,1]. A policy sees only its past actions and signals. Let v=sup_σ E_σ[limsup_n n⁻¹Σ\_{t=1}^n r_t].

**Source.** Raimundo J. Saona Urmeneta. *Robustness of solutions in game theory: Values and strategies in partially observable, perturbed, stochastic, and infinite games*. 2025. Advisor(s): Krishnendu Chatterjee. [primary source](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/download/20234/20240/2025_Saona_Raimundo_Thesis.pdf) Location: §2.4.2, p. 12; Definition 2.4.1, p. 11.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2185, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution found in targeted searches.

**Further links.** [1](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/record/20234) · [2](https://saona-raimundo.github.io/publications/)


<a id="q2186"></a>

## Q2186. Logarithmic second-fire record times

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.1) · **Collection** 22

For every fixed θ>0, does a deterministic c≥1 exist such that almost surely f_{m_i,2}≤c log m_i for all sufficiently large i?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2186: Satyaki Bhattacharya. Random Walks and Forest Fire Models: Recurrence, Transience and Phase Transitions. PhD, Lund University, 2026. Advisor: . Origin for question 2186: Authors’ conjecture. Setup for question 2186: Initially Z_+ is vacant. Each vacant nonburning site grows a tree after an independent Exp(1) time. Trees at 0 ignite immediately; others ignite instantly whenever their left neighbor is burning. Each burns for fixed θ>0, then becomes vacant. Let f_{x,j} be the jth ignition time at x; m_i are the increasing record rightmost sites reached by successive fires from 0.

**Source.** Satyaki Bhattacharya and Stanislav Volkov. *Forest fire model on Z_+ with delays, Journal of Applied Probability, 2026; preprint 2024*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jpr.2025.10040) Location: Journal Conjecture 4.1; preprint v2 Conjecture 4.4, p. 15.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2186, checked 6 October 2026: journal Conjecture 4.1 retains this bound.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.13419) · [2](https://portal.research.lu.se/en/publications/random-walks-and-forest-fire-models-recurrence-transience-and-pha/)


<a id="q2187"></a>

## Q2187. Polylogarithmic Paley clique size

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 22

Do absolute C,K>0 satisfy ω(G_p)≤C(log p)^K for every prime p≡1 mod 4?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2187, 2188: Clique conjecture predates this paper; polynomial SOS improvement is the authors’ §6 conjecture. Setup for questions 2187, 2188: For prime p≡1 mod 4, G_p has vertices F_p and edges where differences are nonzero squares. SOS₄(G) maximizes Σ\_i L(x_i) over linear L on degree≤4 polynomials, with L(1)=1, L(q²)≥0 for deg q≤2, and L vanishing on all degree≤4 multiples of x_i²−x_i and x_ix_j for distinct nonadjacent i,j.

**Source.** Dmitriy Kunisky and Xifan Yu. *A degree 4 sum-of-squares lower bound for the clique number of the Paley graph, CCC 2023; v2, 2024*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.02713) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2187, 2188, checked 6 October 2026: v2 and March 2026 restatement retain both targets.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Global degree-two clique theta, even with entrywise nonnegativity, equals sqrt(p). All 29 eligible prime clique numbers p<=300 exactly certified. On the mean-zero space, the completed multiplicative-character basis is flat relative to nontrivial additive characters; exact-support uncertainty is vacuous for nonconstant rational functions, including centered indicators. Restricted scope: Finite computations restrict p to all primes <=300 congruent to 1 modulo 4. Remaining: The original asymptotic quantifiers are not established. Recorded from the external B2 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected exact checks in the intake; verification archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/B2_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B2-R3: auxiliary proof submission): The two completed orthonormal character bases on the (p-1)-dimensional mean-zero space have overlap magnitude 1/sqrt(p-1); rs>=p-1. Every nonconstant rational function has full nonzero additive Fourier support. Location: Part A, section 3. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).
- **Related result** (B2-PAL-1: auxiliary proof submission): For every prime p congruent to 1 mod 4, global clique theta, global Schrijver clique theta, and degree-2 SOS all equal sqrt(p). Location: Part B; paley_report.md, section 2. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).

<a id="q2188"></a>

## Q2188. Polynomial gain from degree-four SOS

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 22

Does some ε>0 satisfy SOS₄(G_p)=O(p^{1/2−ε}) as p→∞ through primes p≡1 mod 4?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2187, 2188: Clique conjecture predates this paper; polynomial SOS improvement is the authors’ §6 conjecture. Setup for questions 2187, 2188: For prime p≡1 mod 4, G_p has vertices F_p and edges where differences are nonzero squares. SOS₄(G) maximizes Σ\_i L(x_i) over linear L on degree≤4 polynomials, with L(1)=1, L(q²)≥0 for deg q≤2, and L vanishing on all degree≤4 multiples of x_i²−x_i and x_ix_j for distinct nonadjacent i,j.

**Source.** Dmitriy Kunisky and Xifan Yu. *A degree 4 sum-of-squares lower bound for the clique number of the Paley graph, CCC 2023; v2, 2024*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.02713) Location: §6, p. 57, paragraph after Figure 1.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2187, 2188, checked 6 October 2026: v2 and March 2026 restatement retain both targets.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Proved SOS4(G_p)<=1+theta(complement(L_p)), where L_p is the one-local residue graph. Ordinary one-local rational Fourier duals give certified finite SOS4 upper bounds for every eligible p<=300. On the mean-zero space, the completed multiplicative-character basis is flat relative to nontrivial additive characters; exact-support uncertainty is vacuous for nonconstant rational functions, including centered indicators. Restricted scope: Finite computations restrict p to all primes <=300 congruent to 1 modulo 4. Remaining: The original asymptotic quantifiers are not established. Recorded from the external B2 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected exact checks in the intake; verification archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/B2_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B2-R3: auxiliary proof submission): The two completed orthonormal character bases on the (p-1)-dimensional mean-zero space have overlap magnitude 1/sqrt(p-1); rs>=p-1. Every nonconstant rational function has full nonzero additive Fourier support. Location: Part A, section 3. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).
- **Related result** (B2-PAL-2: auxiliary proof submission): For every finite vertex-transitive graph G, SOS4(G)<=1+theta(complement of its neighborhood graph). In particular this holds for every Paley G_p. Location: Part B; paley_report.md, section 3. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).
- **Related result** (B2-PAL-4: auxiliary proof submission): Exact finite results for all 29 primes p<=300, p congruent to 1 mod4, as enumerated in verification.json. Location: Part B; finite experiment. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).

<a id="q2191"></a>

## Q2191. Near-linear sparsity for the Paley frame

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 25) · **Collection** 22

Do absolute C,α>0 and 0<δ<√2−1 exist such that (1−δ)||x||₂²≤||Φ\_px||₂²≤(1+δ)||x||₂² for every x supported on at most Cp/(log p)^α coordinates and all sufficiently large such p?

**Context.** Origin for question 2191: Authors’ Conjecture 25, stronger than merely crossing square-root sparsity. Setup for question 2191: For prime p≡1 mod 4, form Φ\_p with rows {0}∪Q, Q the nonzero quadratic residues, and columns F_p∪{∞}. Its entries are Φ(0,j)=p⁻¹/², Φ(q,j)=√(2/p)e^{2πiqj/p}, and Φ(·,∞)=e₀.

**Source.** Afonso S. Bandeira. *Matthew Fickus, Dustin G. Mixon and Percy Wong, The road to deterministic matrices with the restricted isometry property, 2013*. 2013. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1202.1234) Location: Conjecture 25, p. 20; §5 frame construction.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2191, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution found in targeted searches.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Proved delta_k=(k-1)/sqrt(p) if and only if k<=omega(G_p)+1. For k>=omega+2, proved delta_k<=min(1,(k-4+sqrt(k^2+4k-12))/(2sqrt(p))). Fixed-delta Q2191 must be distinguished from polynomially decaying RIP and stronger quantitative profiles in Satake. On the mean-zero space, the completed multiplicative-character basis is flat relative to nontrivial additive characters; exact-support uncertainty is vacuous for nonconstant rational functions, including centered indicators. Restricted scope: The coherence-saturation and first-gap theorems hold for every eligible prime and finite support size. Computations restrict p<=300. No near-linear sparsity estimate is proved. Remaining: The original asymptotic quantifiers are not established. Recorded from the external B2 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected exact checks in the intake; verification archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/B2_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B2-R3: auxiliary proof submission): The two completed orthonormal character bases on the (p-1)-dimensional mean-zero space have overlap magnitude 1/sqrt(p-1); rs>=p-1. Every nonconstant rational function has full nonzero additive Fourier support. Location: Part A, section 3. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).
- **Related result** (B2-PAL-3: auxiliary proof submission): For every eligible p and integer 1<=k<=p+1, delta_k=(k-1)/sqrt(p) iff k<=omega(G_p)+1. If k>=omega+2 then delta_k<=min(1,(k-4+sqrt(k^2+4k-12))/(2sqrt(p))). Location: Part B; paley_report.md, section 4. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 31) · **Collection** 22

**Context.** Origin for question 2192: Petar Nizić-Nikolac, blog Problem 31, December 2025. Setup for question 2192: For a fixed finite simple graph α with fixed ordered partition V(α)=U⊔V, take independent uniform signs ε\_{ij}=ε\_{ji} for i&lt;j≤n. Set M_α=Σ\_φ(∏\_{ab∈E(α)}ε\_{φ(a)φ(b)})e_{φ(U)}e_{φ(V)}ᵀ, summing over injective φ:V(α)→[n]; rows and columns are indexed by ordered distinct labels.

## Q2193. Subcriticality with sparse transverse streets

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 13) · **Collection** 22

For every fixed a,λ>0, is the occupied set almost surely nonpercolating for all sufficiently small b>0?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2193, 2194, 2195: Authors’ Conjectures 13–14. Setup for questions 2193, 2194, 2195: Fix r>0. Independent Poisson processes of rates a,b on R generate vertical/horizontal lines. On each line put an independent rate-λ Poisson process, and union open radius-r disks at its points. Let λ\_c(a,b) be the occupied percolation threshold and I_c=sup_{μ>0}2μλ\_c(μ,μ). Let λ\_P(r) be the planar homogeneous Poisson radius-r disk threshold.

## Q2199. Finite private memory for every stochastic game

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 22

For every game and ε>0, is there a finite-private-memory strategy that is both uniform and liminf ε-optimal, allowing time-dependent updates/actions?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2199, 2200: Private-memory origin: authors’ 2021 absorbing-games paper. Setup for questions 2199, 2200: Consider finite two-player zero-sum stochastic games with simultaneous actions, observed states/actions, bounded rewards and transition kernels. At time t, action selection uses (t,state,memory); randomized memory updates use current memory, actions and next state. Let v be the initial-state value. Uniform ε-optimal means expected averages≥v−ε against every opponent for all sufficiently large horizons; liminf ε-optimal means expected liminf averages≥v−ε. Private memory is hidden from the opponent; public memory is observed.

**Source.** Kristoffer Arnsfelt Hansen. *Rasmus Ibsen-Jensen and Abraham Neyman, Stochastic Games with Limited Public Memory, 2025*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02623) Location: §6, first open question, p. 20.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2199, 2200, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution found.

## Q2285. Convergence beyond front tightness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 23

For 2/3<α<2, does M_t−[√2t−ct^a/√2−(3/(2√2)−α/(2√2(2+α)))log t] converge in distribution as t→∞?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2285: see question 2284. Origin for question 2285: Geldbach’s Oxford DPhil 2025, supervisors Julien Berestycki and Matthias Winkel; latest paper §1.4. Setup for question 2285: Start binary branching standard Brownian motion at 0∈R², with splitting rate b(θ), continuous, nonnegative and 2π-periodic, uniquely maximized at θ=0, with b(θ)=1−β|θ|^α+O(θ²), β>0. Let M_t=max_u|X_u(t)|, a=(2−α)/(2+α), c=λ₀(2+α)β^{2/(2+α)}/((2−α)2^{2α/(2+α)}), where λ₀=inf spec(−d²/dx²+|x|^α) on L²(R).

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 23

Origin for question 2286: Seiler’s Göttingen PhD 2021, supervisor Anja Sturm; authors’ §3.2. Setup for question 2286: Let G=(V,E) be infinite, connected, vertex- and edge-transitive, with bounded degree and distance d. For every unordered pair e, p_e∈[0,1] and v_e>0 depend only on d(e), with Σ\_y v_{xy}p_{xy}<∞ and Σ\_y v_{xy}^{−1}<∞. Independently, edges refresh at rate γv_e to Bernoulli(qp_e); start edges stationary. Infected vertices recover at fixed r>0 and infect across each open edge at rate λ. Let λ\_c(γ,q) be the survival threshold from one infection; γ=0 means frozen stationary edges.

**Source.** Marco Seiler and Anja Sturm. *Contact process on a dynamical long range percolation, EJP 28*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.08907) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 6.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2286, checked 6 October 2026: targeted searches found no subsequent resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://d-nb.info/1247565378/34)

## Q2287. Ordering count-based game values

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 23

For every n≥1 and p∈[0,1], is P_n^Ab(p)≤P_n^ab(p)≤P_n^aB(p)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2287, 2288, 2289: Authors’ open questions; underlying tree game is Pearl’s 1980 model. Setup for questions 2287, 2288, 2289: Alice and Bob alternate n binary choices each, Alice first; they know all terminal payoffs. Independently label each distinct terminal outcome a Bob-win with probability p. In game Ab, an outcome records Alice’s word and Bob’s number of 1s; aB reverses these roles; ab records both counts. Let P_n^s(p) be the probability Bob can force a win in game s. Let p_c^Ab be its proved 0/1 limiting threshold.

**Source.** Natalia Cardona-Tobón. *Anja Sturm and Jan M. Swart, A min-max random game on a graph that is not a tree*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.02660) Location: After Proposition 1, p. 2; §1.4, p. 12.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2287, 2288, 2289, checked 6 October 2026: targeted searches found no subsequent resolution.

## Q2290. One-label hypergraph spectral gap

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.2) · **Collection** 23

Must λ₂(L_IP)=λ₂(L_RW) for every such weighted hypergraph?

**Context.** Origin for question 2290: Caputo’s hypergraph conjecture, Conjecture 1.2. Setup for question 2290: For n≥2 and nonnegative weights w_B on B⊆[n], put independent rate-w_B clocks on the subsets. On a ring, uniformly permute the labels at sites B. Let L_IP be minus this interchange generator on S_n and L_RW its one-label projection. Write λ₂ for the second-smallest eigenvalue, counting multiplicity.

**Source.** Gil Alon. *Gady Kozma and Doron Puder, On the Aldous-Caputo Spectral Gap Conjecture for Hypergraphs, MPCPS179*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.02505) Location: Conjecture 1.2, pp. 2–3.

- **Partial result**: Gap equality for hyperedges restricted to pairs and the full vertex set, including every n<=3 instance. Scope: Restrict effective hyperedges to pairs and the full vertex set. Assumptions: Restrict effective hyperedges to pairs and the full vertex set. Restrictions/extensions: Restrict effective hyperedges to pairs and the full vertex set. Remaining: Arbitrary weighted hypergraphs outside the stated restricted family. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. **Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.7) · **Collection** 23

<a id="q2293"></a>

## Q2293. Quadratic Brownian-energy gap

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 23

For every such α and w, is the spectral gap attained by a nonconstant polynomial in η of degree at most two?

**Context.** Origin for question 2293: Authors’ §1.4.6, following Kim–Sau’s one-particle and vanishing-diffusivity results. Setup for question 2293: For n≥2, α\_x>0 and symmetric weights w_{xy}≥0 whose positive edges connect [n], let Ω={η≥0:Ση\_x=1}. The Brownian energy process is Dir(α)-reversible with generator Lf=½Σ\_{x,y}w_{xy}[−(α\_yη\_x−α\_xη\_y)(∂\_x−∂\_y)+η\_xη\_y(∂\_x−∂\_y)²]f. Its spectral gap is the bottom of the nonconstant spectrum of −L in L²(Dir(α)).

**Source.** Pietro Caputo. *Matteo Quattropani and Federico Sau, Aldous’ spectral gap phenomena in stochastic exchange models*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10450) Location: §1.4.6, pp. 19–20; displayed BEP generator.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2293, checked 6 October 2026: September 2026 source explicitly leaves this degree-two reduction open.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Common positive edge weights on the complete graph: gap=c sum(alpha), attained in degree one for all positive alpha. Scope: Complete graph with a common positive edge weight; all positive parameter vectors are retained. This includes every connected two-vertex instance. Assumptions: Complete graph with a common positive edge weight; all positive parameter vectors are retained. This includes every connected two-vertex instance. Restrictions/extensions: Complete graph with a common positive edge weight; all positive parameter vectors are retained. This includes every connected two-vertex instance. Remaining: General connected edge weights with arbitrary positive parameter vectors. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/B13_Research_Report.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-026-05728-9)

## Q2294. Monotonic speed on leafless trees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 23

For every such offspring law, is v nonincreasing on [0,m)?

**Context.** Origin for question 2294: Lyons–Pemantle–Peres, 1996/1997 monotonicity question. Setup for question 2294: Let T be a Galton–Watson tree with P(ν=0)=0 and mean m=Eν∈(1,∞). Starting at its root, a λ-biased walk at a vertex with k children chooses its parent with probability λ/(λ+k), each child with probability 1/(λ+k); the root chooses a child uniformly. Let v(λ)=lim_n |X_n|/n, with root-distance |·|.

**Source.** Madhulatha Mandarapu and Sandeep Kunkunuru. *A Computer-Assisted Proof of Speed Monotonicity for the Biased Random Walk on a Galton–Watson Tree Beyond the Known Range, v2*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29894) Location: §1, p. 1; §2, p. 2.

## Q2298. Stochastic advantage of collaborating walks

**Status:** Solved here: disproved · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.6) · **Collection** 23

For every k≥2, positive integer lifespans t_i, and y>0, is P(|⋃\_{i=1}^kR_i(t_i)|≥y)≥P(|R(Σ\_it_i)|≥y)?

**Context.** Origin for question 2298: Authors’ Question 3.6; stronger than their expectation comparison. Setup for question 2298: Let X,X₁,…,X_k be independent copies of the same finite irreducible aperiodic reversible discrete-time Markov chain, all starting independently in its stationary law π. Put R_i(t)={X_i(s):0≤s≤t} and R(t)={X(s):0≤s≤t}.

**Source.** Partha S. Dey. *Daesung Kim and Grigory Terlov, Collaboration of Random Walks on Graphs, arXiv 2023; Proc.AMS153*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.14241) Location: Question 3.6, p. 11; AssumptionI, pp. 2–3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2298, checked 6 October 2026: targeted searches found no subsequent resolution.

**Results.**

- **Disproved**: A seven-state half-lazy reversible birth-death chain with bias ratio r=20 gives P(two stationary three-step ranges cover)/P(one stationary six-step range covers)=87400/160401<1. The ratio tends to zero as r grows. Remaining: No stochastic dominance is claimed from the separate expected-range theorem. Transitive or narrower model classes were not resolved. Recorded from the external B5 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected local controls in the intake; external archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b5-2026-10-07/B5_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 24

<a id="q2395"></a>

## Q2395. Stationary law of the drift-jump system

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 24

Give an explicit description of the unique stationary distribution of this infinite particle system.

**Context.** Origin for question 2395: Unpublished 1993 notes; Aldous–Diaconis 1999, §5.2. Setup for question 2395: For each k, particles 0&lt;X₁<⋯&lt;X_k obey dX_i/dt=X_i. At every point (x,t) of a unit-intensity Poisson process on (0,∞)², move the nearest particle strictly right of x to x, if one exists. The k-particle laws form a consistent infinite system.

**Source.** David Aldous. *A one-dimensional drift-jump particle process*. 2018. [primary source](https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/left_Hamm.html) Location: Problem paragraph and preceding two paragraphs.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2395, checked 6 October 2026: no later explicit stationary-law solution found.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: A complete constructive finite-dimensional stationary law is given by Poissonized Plancherel growth and an explicit nonnegative skew-tableau sum, with a Poisson-tail error bound at computable thresholds. Remaining: A compact all-particle joint density, general gap factorization, or finite multipoint determinant has not been established. Registry status is deliberately conservative. Recorded from the external B5 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected local controls in the intake; external archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b5-2026-10-07/B5_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).


<a id="q2396"></a>

## Q2396. Concavity under recursive overlap

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.1) · **Collection** 24

Is H_n concave on [0,1] for every such sequence and every n≥1?

**Context.** Origin for question 2396: Author’s Conjecture 3.1. Setup for question 2396: For integers 2=a₁&lt;a₂<⋯, put P₁(p)=(p,1−p), P_{n+1}(p)=p(P_n(p),0,…,0)+(1−p)(0,…,0,P_n(p)) in R^{a_{n+1}}. Set H_n(p)=−Σ\_jP_{n,j}(p)log P_{n,j}(p), with 0log0=0.

**Source.** Jörg Neunhäuserer. *Recursive overlap Bernoulli distributions and an entropy concavity conjecture, v2*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05546) Location: Conjecture 3.1, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2396, checked 6 October 2026: September 22 v2 retains common-p concavity; the September 15 counterexample uses unequal Bernoulli parameters.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Strict entropy concavity is proved for every four positive real weights by an exhaustive exact computer-assisted proof; one to three weights have elementary proofs. Arbitrarily large separated blocks of at most four also satisfy concavity. Restricted scope: The universal-n target is restricted to n<=4, or to a specified injectively encoded separated-block class; Within the n=4 theorem the weight domain is enlarged from positive integers with w_1=1 to arbitrary positive real weights. Remaining: General interior concavity for n>=5 remains unresolved. The certificate is not a kernel-checked formal proof. Recorded from the external B5 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected local controls in the intake; external archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b5-2026-10-07/B5_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).

## Q2397. Uniform dependence on greedy-walk start

**Status:** Solved here: disproved · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4) · **Collection** 24

Is sup_G max_v L(G,v)/min_v L(G,v)<∞, over all such graphs with at least two vertices?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2397, 2398: Author’s Problem 4 and §§3–4.1 conjectures. Setup for questions 2397, 2398: For a finite connected positively weighted graph, use shortest-path distance d. Assume unique choices. Starting at v, repeatedly go to the nearest unvisited vertex; L(G,v) is total distance until all vertices are visited, without returning. Random models below start uniformly over vertices.

**Source.** David Aldous. *The Nearest Unvisited Vertex Walk on Random Graphs, v2*. 2021. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.13175) Location: Problem 4, p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2397, 2398, checked 6 October 2026: targeted searches found no resolution.

**Results.**

- **Disproved**: Explicit positive weighted trees of degree at most three have a start ratio growing as h/4 with n=2^(h+1)-1. An explicit rational perturbation makes all pair distances distinct. A general harmonic upper bound proves worst-case order Theta(log N). Remaining: No sharp leading constant for the maximum start ratio is claimed; no historical-priority claim is made. Recorded from the external B5 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected local controls in the intake; external archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. <a id="q2399"></a>

## Q2399. Rooted common-subtree scaling law

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 24

Does (n^(−1/2)τ\_n,n^(−1/2)τ′\_n,n^(−1/2)L•(τ\_n,τ′\_n)) converge jointly in distribution to (2T/σ,2T′/σ′,c min{2H(T)/σ,2H(T′)/σ′}), with Gromov–Hausdorff convergence in the first two coordinates?

**Context.** Origin for question 2399: Authors’ §6(i), using Theorem 1.2 hypotheses. Setup for question 2399: Let τ\_n,τ′\_n be independent critical nondegenerate Bienaymé trees of offspring laws μ,μ′, conditioned on n vertices (admissible n). Their variances σ²,σ′² are finite, and μ has a finite (2+κ)-moment, κ>0. L• counts vertices in a largest root-preserving common subtree, ignoring order/labels. Let τ\*,τ′\* have root offspring laws (k+1)μ(k+1),(k+1)μ′(k+1), with ordinary independent μ,μ′ descendants; set c=E L•(τ\*,τ′\*). T,T′ are independent Brownian CRTs coded by normalized Brownian excursions; H denotes root-height.

**Source.** Omer Angel. *Caelan Atamanchuk, Anna Brandenberger, Serte Donderwinkel and Robin Khanfir, The largest common subtree of two random trees*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00119) Location: §6(i), p. 41; Theorem 1.2 definitions, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2399, checked 6 October 2026: current v1 retains this rooted extension.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: L_n^bullet=c min(H(tau_n),H(tau_prime_n))+o_P(sqrt(n)), giving the exact asserted joint CRT limit and rooted GH convergence. Scope: Independent critical nondegenerate Bienayme trees conditioned on common admissible size n; finite positive variances sigma^2,sigma_prime^2, and a finite (2+kappa)-moment for mu. Common subtrees preserve the original roots and ignore order and labels. c=E L_bullet(tau_star,tau_prime_star) for root offspring (k+1)mu(k+1) and (k+1)mu_prime(k+1). Independent normalized-excursion Brownian CRTs. Restrictions/extensions: No narrowing: rooted GH convergence of the tree coordinates is proved in addition to the retained unmarked conclusion. Remaining: Uses Proposition 4.3 uniform estimates from the primary source. No mathematical gap identified in the independent written reviews; no formal verification. Filed from the external B6 return; scope reading and selected controls do not constitute an independent full proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b6-2026-10-08/B6_Research_Report_2026-10-08.md).


<a id="q2400"></a>

## Q2400. Log-concave high-degree random independent sets

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.1) · **Collection** 24

For every sufficiently large fixed d, does P(x_k(G)²≥x_{k−1}(G)x_{k+1}(G) for every k≥1)→1 as n→∞?

**Context.** Origin for question 2400: Author’s high-degree log-concavity question. Setup for question 2400: Let G∼G(n,d/n) and x_k(G) count its independent k-vertex sets, with x₀=1 and x_k=0 beyond the independence number.

**Source.** Steven Heilman. *Independent Sets of Random Trees and Sparse Random Graphs, JGT 109*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1002/jgt.23225) Location: Journal §8 Question 8.1; preprint Question 1.21.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2400, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution found for the random-graph question; deterministic-tree log-concavity is refuted.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: For fixed d>0 and n>=max(2,d), simultaneously x_k^2>=x_(k-1)x_(k+1) for 1<=k<=min(n-1,floor(((n-1)/(2d))^(1/3))) with probability at least 1-exp(-d(n-1)/6). Also an exact positive expectation formula for every defect. Remaining: The full coefficient row with high probability remains unresolved. Positive expectation alone does not imply a high-probability sign. Recorded from the external B3 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); initial argument review and bounded independent controls in the intake; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b3-2026-10-06/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.04756)

## Q2493. Small-exponent branching slowdown

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For 0<α≤2/3, is M_t=√2t−ct^a(1+o_P(1))/√2 as t→∞?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2493: see question 2492. Origin for question 2493: Authors’ stated residual question. Setup for question 2493: Start binary branching standard Brownian motion at 0∈R², with splitting rate b(θ), continuous, nonnegative and 2π-periodic, uniquely maximized at θ=0, with b(θ)=1−β|θ|^α+O(θ²), β>0. Let M_t=max_u|X_u(t)|, a=(2−α)/(2+α), c=λ₀(2+α)β^{2/(2+α)}/((2−α)2^{2α/(2+α)}), where λ₀=inf spec(−d²/dx²+|x|^α) on L²(R).

**Source.** Julien Berestycki; David Geldbach; Michel Pain. *Polynomial slowdown in an angle-dependent 2d branching Brownian motion*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10623) Location: §1.4(ii), p. 9, α≤2/3 paragraph.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2493, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026: question retained in the reviewed source; targeted searches found no matching resolution.

## Q2494. Optimal connected-complement gap exponent

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Determine the smallest p for which some c_p>0 satisfies gap(G)+gap(Ḡ)≥c_p n^{−p} for every n-vertex G with both G and Ḡ connected.

Write Ḡ for the simple complement.

**Source.** Sooyeong Kim; Neal Madras. *A Nordhaus–Gaddum problem for the spectral gap of a graph*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15167) Location: Introduction, p. 2, paragraph following Theorem 1.4.

<a id="q2495"></a>

## Q2495. Memory-tree height fluctuations

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For each fixed β∈(0,1), determine the scale and limiting distribution of H_n−E H_n, including the transition at β=1/2.

**Context.** Origin for question 2495: Authors’ stated question. Setup for question 2495: Start with vertex 1. For fixed β∈(0,1), vertex n+1 attaches uniformly to {max(1,n−⌊n^β⌋),…,n}. Equip the resulting T_n with graph distance; H_n is its height from vertex 1.

**Source.** Omer Angel; Shankar Bhamidi; Serte Donderwinkel; Neeladri Maitra; Akshay Sakanaveeti. *Evolution of recursive trees with limited memory*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18856) Location: §3.3(c), p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2495, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026: question retained in the reviewed source; targeted searches found no matching resolution.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: For every fixed 0&lt;beta<1/2, Var H_n ~ 2 n^(1-beta)/(3(1-beta)) and the corresponding centered normal limit. The same depth theorem holds for all fixed 0&lt;beta<1; ||H_n-D_n||\_2=O(n^(beta/2) log n). Exact clock F(n) is a valid centering. Scope: Root 1; vertex n+1 independently chooses a uniform parent in {max(1,n-floor(n^beta)),...,n}; fixed beta in (0,1), full-tree root height H_n, centered by E H_n. Restrictions/extensions: No model change. Height theorem restricted to fixed beta in (0,1/2); the auxiliary single-vertex depth theorem covers fixed beta in (0,1). Remaining: Critical and supercritical height scales and distributions remain. The all-beta auxiliary theorem concerns D_n, not H_n. Filed from the external B6 return; scope reading and selected controls do not constitute an independent full proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b6-2026-10-08/B6_Research_Report_2026-10-08.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://www.sertedonderwinkel.com/)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.9) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

## Q2585. Fleming–Viot non-explosion

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Must the successive rebirth times tend to infinity almost surely for every d≥1, D and N as above?

**Context.** Origin for question 2585: Thesis §2.2 reviews the general non-explosion problem; the Brownian-domain case is reaffirmed in the follow-on paper §5. Setup for question 2585: Let D⊂R^d be a bounded connected open set, with no boundary regularity assumed. Start N≥3 particles at arbitrary points of D. They move as independent standard Brownian motions; whenever one hits ∂D, it immediately jumps to the position of a uniformly chosen other particle and continues independently.

**Source.** Oliver Tough. *Asymptotic Behaviour of the Fleming-Viot Process*. 2021. Advisor(s): James Nolen. [primary source](https://dukespace.lib.duke.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/b013536b-8438-495a-a64a-4d42c7bf1499/content) Location: Thesis §2.2, pp. 14–16; follow-on 2110.05049v2, §5, p. 27.

## Q2586. ASEP circle mixing without cutoff

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 26

For every fixed ε∈(0,1), is t_N(ε)=Θ\_{p,ε}(N² min{k,N−k}^{−1/2}), with no total-variation cutoff for any such sequence k(N)? Here cutoff means t_N(ε)/t_N(1−ε)→1 for all ε∈(0,1/2).

**Context.** Origin for question 2586: Hubert Lacoin’s conjecture, attributed to personal communication. Setup for question 2586: Fix p∈(1/2,1). On Z/NZ, k=k(N)∈{1,…,N−1} particles jump clockwise at rate p and counterclockwise at rate 1−p, only into vacant sites. Let t_N(ε) be worst-start total-variation mixing time to the uniform law on k-particle configurations.

**Source.** Dominik Schmid; Allan Sly. *Mixing times for the TASEP on the circle*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00440-026-01466-0) Location: §1.4, Conjecture 1.3; mixing convention (1.4)–(1.5).

## Q2594. Distance-universal quadratic rendezvous

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Is there one sequence a, independent of d, and C<∞ such that E T_d(a)≤Cd² for every integer d≥1?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2594, 2595: Authors’ §5 questions. Setup for questions 2594, 2595: Two agents start at −d,d∈Z and move synchronously one unit per round. Both use the same sequence a_i∈[0,1]: at distance i≥1 from their own start they move away with probability a_i, otherwise toward it; at their start they choose either direction fairly. Choices are independent. T_d(a) is their first common-vertex time, and R_d=inf_a E T_d(a).

**Source.** Konstantinos Georgiou; Claude Gravel; Joey Kapusin; Lazar Mandic. *Oblivious Self-Distance Symmetric Rendezvous on the Integer Line*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13632) Location: §5, p. 26; full model §2.1, pp. 4–5.

## Q2683. Entropy criterion for perturbed-graph cutoff

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 7.1) · **Collection** 27

If h_n(v)≍h_n uniformly in n,v and h_n=o(log N_n), does t_n(δ)≍log N_n/(ε\_n h_n) hold with high probability, with t_n(δ)/t_n(1−δ)→1 in probability for every δ∈(0,1/2)?

**Context.** Origin for question 2683: Conjecture 7.1; doctoral project of Baran and Šarković, supervised by Perla Sousi. Setup for question 2683: Let G_n be connected finite graphs of even orders N_n→∞ and uniformly bounded degrees. Add a uniformly random perfect matching with edge weight ε\_n∈(0,1), keeping original edges of weight 1 and parallel edges. The discrete-time walk chooses incident edges proportionally to weight. Conditional on the resulting graph, let h_n(v) be the Shannon entropy of X_{τ\_n} under start v, where τ\_n is its first step traversing an added edge; set h_n=N_n⁻¹∑\_v h_n(v). Let t_n(δ) be worst-start total-variation mixing time.

**Source.** Zsuzsanna Baran; Jonathan Hermon; Anđela Šarković; Perla Sousi. *Phase transition for random walks on graphs with added weighted random matching*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.13077) Location: §7.1, Conjecture 7.1, p. 66.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2683, checked 7 October 2026: No resolution located in bounded searches through 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/handle/1810/392322) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00440-024-01342-9)

## Q2684. Subcritical small-world cutoff

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 27

Does the walk have cutoff at logarithmic time: t_n(δ)=Θ(log n) and t_n(δ)/t_n(1−δ)→1 in probability for each δ∈(0,1/2)?

**Context.** Origin for question 2684: Authors’ subcritical-exponent conjecture. Setup for question 2684: Fix d≥3 and 0≤r&lt;d. On the nearest-neighbor torus (Z/nZ)^d, independently add each unordered pair {x,y} as an edge with probability Z_n/dist(x,y)^r, where dist is torus graph distance and Z_n makes every vertex’s expected added degree 1. Retain parallel edges. Run discrete-time simple random walk, choosing an incident edge uniformly; t_n(δ) denotes worst-start total-variation mixing time.

**Source.** Zsuzsanna Baran; Jonathan Hermon; Anđela Šarković; Allan Sly; Perla Sousi. *Random walk on the small-world network model in 3 or more dimensions*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12056) Location: §1, p. 2; Definition 1.1, p. 1.

## Q2685. Fast analytic simulation on compact sets

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 27

Does such an algorithm always output j with probability f_j(p), with its input-roll count N satisfying P_p(N>n)≤C_p ρ\_p^n for some C_p<∞ and ρ\_p<1, for every p∈K?

**Context.** Origin for question 2685: Thesis §3.6 extends the rectangular-domain result sought after Theorem 3.19. Setup for question 2685: Let Δ\_m={p∈(0,1)^(m+1):∑p_i=1}, with m,v≥1. Let S⊂Δ\_m be relatively open, K⊂S compact, and f:S→Δ\_v real analytic on a neighborhood of K. An algorithm knows f,K but accesses the unknown p∈K only through independent rolls of a p-distributed die; independent auxiliary randomness is allowed.

**Source.** Giulio Morina. *Extending the Bernoulli Factory to a Dice Enterprise*. 2021. Advisor(s): Krzysztof Łatuszyński; Ewan Cameron. [primary source](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/157145/1/WRAP_Theses_Morina_2021.pdf) Location: Definition 3.18, pp. 72–73; §3.6, p. 88.

## Q2687. All-moment bounds for meteor mass flow

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 27

For every α>0, is sup_{t≥0} E|F_t|^α<∞?

**Context.** Origin for question 2687: Author’s strengthening of Theorem 5.1 in Remark 5.3(i). Setup for question 2687: At each site x∈Z, an independent rate-1 Poisson clock redistributes all mass M^x equally to x−1 and x+1, resetting M^x to zero. Let Q∞ be the weak limiting law from unit mass at every site, and start in Q∞. F_t is net mass crossing edge {0,1} toward 1 by time t: half the pre-hit mass at 0, summed over its hits, minus half the pre-hit mass at 1, summed over its hits.

**Source.** Krzysztof Burdzy. *Meteor process on Z^d*. 2015. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.6865) Location: Remark 5.3(i), p. 34; §5, p. 29.

## Q2688. Exact earthworm growth exponent

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.2) · **Collection** 27

Does lim_{n→∞}log E[S_n]/log n exist, and what is its value?

**Context.** Origin for question 2688: Authors’ full growth-exponent problem, rather than its weaker 3/4 lower-bound conjecture. Setup for question 2688: Let X_n be simple random walk on Z², X_0=0, and H_0={0}. At a step X_{n+1}=X_n+e, inspect the ray {X_n+ke:k≥1}. If it contains no point of H_n, set H_{n+1}=H_n∪{X_{n+1}}; otherwise remove its nearest H_n-point and insert X_{n+1}. Let S_n=|H_n|.

**Source.** Krzysztof Burdzy; Shi Feng; Daisuke Shiraishi. *On the size of earthworm’s trail*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.02742) Location: §4.1, Problem 4.2, p. 8; model §2.

## Q2689. Bounded Brownian pieces and reversal

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7.7) · **Collection** 27

Does sup_{k∈Z}T_k<∞ almost surely force X to be backward Brownian?

**Context.** Origin for question 2689: Authors’ Problem 7.7. Setup for question 2689: For k∈Z, let B^k be standard one-dimensional Brownian motions, each stopped at a finite nonnegative stopping time T_k. The stopped pairs are independent, and ∑\_{k≥0}T_k=∑\_{k<0}T_k=∞ almost surely. Set S_0=0, S_{k+1}−S_k=T_k and concatenate into X with X_0=0 and X_{S_k+t}−X_{S_k}=B^k_t for 0≤t&lt;T_k. Call X backward Brownian if random U_n→∞ almost surely exist such that (X_{U_n−t}−X_{U_n})\_{t≥0} is standard Brownian for every n.

**Source.** Krzysztof Burdzy; Michael Scheutzow. *Forward Brownian motion*. 2014. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1302.6958) Location: Problem 7.7, p. 26; Definitions 2.1–2.2, pp. 2–3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2689, checked 7 October 2026: No resolution located in bounded searches through 7 October 2026.

A peak has a larger label than each neighbor. Let K₁,K₂ be their locations, and dist the torus graph distance.

<a id="q2693"></a>

## Q2693. Rigid symmetry behind shy coupling

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 27

If D admits a shy co-adapted coupling, must it admit one of the form Y_t=T(X_t) for a fixed Euclidean isometry T with T(D)=D?

**Context.** Origin for question 2693: Authors’ Conjectures 1–2. Setup for question 2693: Let D⊂R^d, d≥2, be bounded, connected and open. Assume r,δ>0 and α∈(0,π/2] exist such that for every x∈∂D, N_{x,r}={ν:|ν|=1,B(x+rν,r)∩D=∅} is nonempty and equals N_{x,s} for 0&lt;s≤r; also some unit m_x satisfies (y+{z:z·m_x>|z|cosα})∩B(x,δ)⊆D for every y∈D∩B(x,δ). B denotes an open Euclidean ball. A co-adapted coupling consists of normally reflected Brownian motions X,Y, each retaining its reflected-Brownian transition laws relative to their common filtration. It is shy if P(inf_{t≥0}|X_t−Y_t|≥ε)>0 for some ε>0.

**Source.** Maury Bramson; Krzysztof Burdzy; Wilfrid Kendall. *Shy couplings, CAT(0) spaces, and the lion and man*. 2013. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1007.3199) Location: §5.3, Conjecture 2, p. 40.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2693, checked 7 October 2026: The later rubber-band paper covers star-shaped domains; no general resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.0597)

## Q2694. Failure of bounded synchronous coalescence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2.1) · **Collection** 27

**Context.** Origin for questions 2694, 2695: Authors’ Problems 2.1(i) and 2.6. Setup for questions 2694, 2695: For a connected planar domain D with C⁴ boundary, let X,Y be normally reflected Brownian motions from distinct x,y∈D̄ driven by the same Brownian motion B: dX_t=dB_t+n(X_t)dL^X_t and dY_t=dB_t+n(Y_t)dL^Y_t. Here n is the inward normal and each boundary local time increases only when its particle lies on ∂D.

**Source.** Krzysztof Burdzy; Zhen-Qing Chen; Peter Jones. *Synchronous couplings of reflected Brownian motions in smooth domains*. 2006. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0501486) Location: Problem 2.1(i), p. 4; §1 model.

## Q2698. Three-dimensional two-stage threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 27

For d=r=3, is γ₂=3?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2698, 2699: Authors’ §6 questions. Setup for questions 2698, 2699: On Z^d, start from independent states 0,1,2 with probabilities 1−p−q,p,q, where p,q>0 and p+q<1. Updates are simultaneous. In the standard two-stage rule with threshold r, state i∈{0,1} increases to i+1 when at least r nearest neighbors have state i+1; state 2 is permanent. For each d,r, define γ₂=inf{γ>0: every positive q(p)≤p^γ yields P(0 eventually becomes 2)→1 as p↓0}, with inf∅=∞.

**Source.** Zihao Fang; Janko Gravner; David Sivakoff. *Two-stage Bootstrap Percolation*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16541) Location: §6 item 5, p.23 (arXiv v1); current author manuscript §6.5, pp.26–27.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.math.ucdavis.edu/~gravner/papers/repl.pdf)

## Q2736. Infinite mean attraction without degree-two ergodicity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 28

If P is not ergodic of degree 2, must E[T_A(x)]=∞ for every x and every RDS representation?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2736: Robin Chemnitz. Aspects of the Long-Term Evolution of Ergodic Cocycles: The Mather Semigroup, Hyperbolicity and Random Attractors. PhD, Freie Universität Berlin, 2026. Advisors: Maximilian Engel. Origin for question 2736: Conjecture 1, p. 20. Setup for question 2736: Let P be irreducible positive recurrent on countable X, with stationary law π. An RDS representation consists of i.i.d. maps F_n:X→X, n∈Z, with Pr(F_n(x)=y)=P(x,y). Put Φ\_n=F_{n−1}∘⋯∘F₀ and A_n=supp lim_{m→∞}(F_{n−1}∘⋯∘F_{n−m})\_\*π, the finite random attractor. Set T_A(x)=inf{n≥0:Φ\_n(x)∈A_n}. Degree 2 means E_xτ\_x²<∞ for one (equivalently every) x, where τ\_x=inf{n≥1:X_n=x}.

**Source.** Robin Chemnitz, Maximilian Engel and Guillermo Olicón Mendez. *Random attractors on countable state spaces*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19898v2) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 20

**Literature check.** Status for question 2736, checked 7 October 2026: August 2025 v2 retains this conjecture; its older necessity claim was removed. No later exact resolution found by 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1214/25-EJP1384) · [2](https://www.mi.fu-berlin.de/fb/dates/disputationen/Chemnitz_-Robin---MI---Aushang.pdf)

## Q2789. Sharp anisotropic recurrence criterion

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 28

**Context.** Origin for question 2789: Authors’ recurrence/transience conjecture. Setup for question 2789: Start C_0=(0,0) on Z². At height j, move to each vertical neighbour with probability p_j and to each horizontal neighbour with probability 1/2−p_j, where 0&lt;p_j≤1/2 and p_j<1/2 for at least one j.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.6) · **Collection** 28

For every λ>0, must the occupied set contain an infinite connected component with positive probability?

**Context.** Origin for question 2790: Authors’ planar random-ray question. Setup for question 2790: At each x∈Z² independently place Poisson(λ) segments. Each segment independently has length L∈{1,2,…} with E L<∞ and E L²=∞, chooses uniformly one of the four coordinate directions e, and occupies {x,x+e,…,x+(L−1)e}. Connectivity uses nearest-neighbour adjacency of occupied sites.

**Source.** Gábor Pete; Sándor Rokob. *Nonamenable Poisson zoo*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07145) Location: Question 1.6, p. 8.

## Q2791. Loop-erased ants find every geodesic

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 28

Select the chronological loop-erasure of the reversed walk. Must W_e(n)/n converge almost surely to χ\_e, with χ\_e>0 exactly for edges on an N–F shortest path?

**Context.** Origin for question 2791: Authors’ Conjecture 1.1, two distinct reinforcement protocols. Setup for question 2791: On a finite connected loopless undirected multigraph with distinct N,F, start all edge weights at 1. Each successive walk starts at N, chooses incident edges proportionally to their current weights, and stops at F. Afterward add 1 to each edge in its selected return path.

**Source.** Daniel Kious; Cécile Mailler; Bruno Schapira. *Finding geodesics on graphs using reinforcement learning*. 2022. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.04820) Location: Conjecture 1.1(1), pp. 3–4.

## Q2792. Deterministic trace-reinforcement limit

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 28

Must (W_e(n)/n)\_{e∈E} converge almost surely to a deterministic vector?

**Context.** Origin for question 2792: The deterministic-convergence assertion of Conjecture 1.1. Setup for question 2792: On a finite connected loopless undirected multigraph with distinct N,F and no parallel edges incident to F, set W_e(0)=1. Walk n starts at N, chooses incident edges proportionally to W(n−1), and stops at first hitting F. Then increment by 1 every edge traversed at least once.

**Source.** Daniel Kious; Cécile Mailler; Bruno Schapira. *The trace-reinforced ants process does not find shortest paths*. 2022. [primary source](https://www.numdam.org/articles/10.5802/jep.188/) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 507.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 28

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

For every W>0, does this process visit every vertex infinitely often almost surely?

## Q2885. Critical local tree limits

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1.8) · **Collection** 29

For every C>0 and β=Cn, does (T,1) have an annealed local weak limit? Identify these laws, and are they distinct for every two different positive values of C?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2885: Luca Makowiec. PhD thesis, National University of Singapore, 2025. Advisors: Rongfeng Sun. Origin for question 2885: 2025 PhD thesis, National University of Singapore; advisor Rongfeng Sun. Question 4.1.8 sharpens the author’s earlier local-limit question. Setup for question 2885: On K_n, give edges iid U_e∼Uniform[0,1]. Conditional on these labels, sample T with probability proportional to exp(−β∑\_{e∈T}U_e), root it at vertex 1, and forget labels. Local weak convergence means convergence in distribution of every fixed-radius rooted graph ball.

**Source.** Luca Makowiec. *Observables of random spanning trees in random environment*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07702) Location: Thesis Question 4.1.8, p. 48; Definition 4.2.1, p. 49; local-limit paper p. 3.

## Q2886. Spanning-tree diameter crossover

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 29

Under the joint law of U and T, is diam T=n^{a+o(1)} with high probability, where a=1/2 if β\_n≤n^{1+o(1)}, a=(1−γ)/2 if β\_n=n^{1+γ+o(1)} for fixed 0<γ<1/3, and a=1/3 if β\_n≥n^{4/3+o(1)}?

**Context.** Origin for question 2886: Joint-paper conjecture, repeated as Conjecture 5.3.3 in Makowiec’s 2025 NUS PhD thesis, advised by Rongfeng Sun. Setup for question 2886: Let β\_n≥0. Independently label edges e of K_n by U_e∼Uniform[0,1]. Conditional on U, sample a spanning tree T with probability proportional to exp(−β\_n∑\_{e∈T}U_e). Diameter uses graph distance.

**Source.** Luca Makowiec; Michele Salvi; Rongfeng Sun. *Random spanning trees in random environment*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16830v4) Location: Conjecture 1.3, p. 5; thesis Conjecture 5.3.3, p. 77.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2886, checked 7 October 2026: Retained in May 2026 v4; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07702)

## Q2887. A finite-dimensional dependent threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.14) · **Collection** 29

Does some integer d≥3 satisfy p₁,c(Z^d)=4−2√3?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2887, 2888: Authors’ Conjectures 1.14 and 1.18. Setup for questions 2887, 2888: A random spanning subgraph is 1-independent if restrictions to vertex-disjoint edge sets are independent. Let p₁,c(H) be the infimum of p such that every such law with all edge marginals ≥p has an infinite component almost surely. Write Q_n={0,1}^n with Hamming-distance-one edges.

**Source.** Victor Falgas-Ravry; Vincent Pfenninger. *1-independent percolation on Z²×K_n*. 2023. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1002/rsa.21129) Location: Conjecture 1.14, p. 892.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.1) · **Collection** 29

## Q2891. Associated percolation’s leading threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 29

Determine the first-order asymptotic of p_a⁺(Z^d) as d→∞; in particular, does d·p_a⁺(Z^d) converge, and to what constant?

**Context.** Origin for question 2891: Authors’ high-dimensional first-order question. Setup for question 2891: For nearest-neighbour Z^d, let p_a⁺(Z^d) be the supremum of p admitting a nonpercolating 1-independent bond law with every edge marginal ≥p and P(A∩B)≥P(A)P(B) for all increasing cylinder events A,B. Here 1-independence means independence for vertex-disjoint edge sets; nonpercolation means no infinite component almost surely.

**Source.** Laurin Köhler-Schindler; Aurelio L. Sulser. *Critical probabilities for positively associated, finite-range dependent percolation models*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07345) Location: Final discussion, pp. 37–38.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.4) · **Collection** 29

As n→∞, is E T=(1+o(1))(r/s)·binom(n,s)log(binom(n,s))/binom(r,s)?

## Q2893. Supercritical nonlinear-memory transience

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2.10) · **Collection** 29

If h is differentiable near 1/2 and h′(1/2)>1/2, must S_n visit 0 only finitely often almost surely?

**Context.** Origin for question 2893: Authors’ supercritical recurrence/transience problem. Setup for question 2893: Fix p,q∈(0,1), f:[0,1]→[0,1], and h=(1−p)+(2p−1)f. Let S₀=0, P(S₁=1)=q, and P(S_{n+1}−S_n=1|S₁,…,S_n)=h((1+S_n/n)/2). Suppose 1/2 is h’s unique fixed point in (0,1), and sup_{y∈C}(y−1/2)(h(y)−y)<0 for every nonempty closed C⊂(0,1)∖{1/2}.

**Source.** Krishanu Maulik; Parthanil Roy; Tamojit Sadhukhan. *Asymptotic Properties of Generalized Elephant Random Walks*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19383v2) Location: Open Problem 2.10, p. 8; Theorem 2.2; (2.5),(2.9).

## Q2895. Almost-sure Liouville Weyl law

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.5) · **Collection** 29

Does N(λ)/λ→cγA almost surely as λ→∞?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2895, 2896, 2897: Weyl strengthening is posed here; eigenfunction and gap conjectures originate with Berestycki–Wong (2023). Setup for questions 2895, 2896, 2897: Let D⊂C be a bounded simply connected open domain, φ its zero-boundary GFF with logarithmic Green covariance, 0<γ<2, and μγ=lim_{ε↓0}ε^{γ²/2}e^{γφε(x)}dx, with φε the circle average. Let λ\_j↑∞ enumerate, with multiplicity, the full spectrum of the negative generator of killed Liouville Brownian motion (underlying Brownian generator Δ/2); let f_j be a corresponding orthonormal eigenbasis in L²(μγ). Put N(λ)=#{j:λ\_j≤λ}, A=μγ(D), cγ=1/[π(2−γ²/2)].

**Source.** Nathanaël Berestycki. *On the spectral geometry of Liouville quantum gravity*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.02538) Location: Problem 4.5, p. 21.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2895, 2896, 2897, checked 7 October 2026: No resolution of these targets located through 7 October 2026.

## Q2898. Second-order loop-percolation threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 29

Is α\_c(d)=2d−3/2+O(1/d) as d→∞?

Setup for questions 2898, 2899: On nearest-neighbour Z^d, d≥3, independently include Poisson(α/[k(2d)^k]) copies of each based closed walk (x₀,…,x_k=x₀) of length k≥2. Open every traversed edge. Write C(0) for the origin’s cluster, α\_c(d)=inf{α:P(|C(0)|=∞)>0}, and α\_#(d)=sup{α:E|C(0)|<∞}.

**Source.** Luca Makowiec; Artem Sapozhnikov. *Critical curve of loop percolation on the d-regular tree*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27520) Location: Remark 1.5, p. 3; loop measure, p. 1.

## Q2900. Realizing every density as a distance law

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.7) · **Collection** 29

Is every probability law on [0,∞) having a Lebesgue density and 0 in its support feasible?

**Context.** Origin for question 2900: Authors’ density-realization problem. Setup for question 2900: A law on [0,∞) is feasible if it is the distribution of d(X,Y) for independent μ-samples X,Y, where (S,d) is a complete separable metric space and μ is a Borel probability measure.

**Source.** David J. Aldous; Guillaume Blanc; Nicolas Curien. *The distance problem on measured metric spaces*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.5802/afst.1835) Location: Journal Problem 1.7, p. 1347; Gross, Problem 1.3, p. 1025.

## Q2994. Separate comb densities

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

For every λ>0, do ρ\_T(λ)=lim E[T_n]/n and ρ\_B(λ)=lim E[B_n]/n exist and satisfy ρ\_T(λ)<ρ\_B(λ)<ρ\_I(λ)?

**Context.** Origin for question 2994: Joint-paper conjecture. Setup for question 2994: C_n has spine 1,…,n, one leaf at each spine vertex, and absorbing spine endpoints 0,n+1. Active particles perform rate-one simple random walks; an isolated particle sleeps at rate λ>0 and wakes on contact with an active particle. Stabilize one active particle at every nonsink vertex. Let T_n and B_n count final sleepers on leaves and spine. Let ρ\_I(λ) be the limiting expected sleepers per site for the same process on an interval.

**Source.** Matthew Junge; Josh Meisel; Aldo Morelli. *Activated random walk on the comb*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.14977v2) Location: §1.3, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2994, checked 7 October 2026: September 2025 v2 retains it; no later resolution located.

## Q2995. Speed with leaves in causal maps

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

Does simple random walk X_n from ρ satisfy d_{C(T)}(ρ,X_n)/n→v_μ almost surely for some deterministic v_μ>0, without assuming μ(0)=0?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2995, 2996: Thomas Budzinski. Cartes aléatoires hyperboliques. PhD in mathematics, Université Paris-Saclay; Université Paris-Sud registration, 2018. Setup for questions 2995, 2996: Let μ be an offspring law on N₀ with mean m>1, possibly infinite. Condition its plane Galton–Watson tree T to survive. Form C(T) by joining consecutive vertices at each generation cyclically. Edges, loops and multiplicities retain the map convention; ρ is the tree root.

**Source.** Thomas Budzinski. *Supercritical causal maps: geodesics and simple random walk*. 2019. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.10588v2) Location: §5, p. 44; thesis §5.5, p. 163.

## Q2999. Polynomial repelling-walk mixing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.1) · **Collection** 30

For every fixed G and β<0, is t_mix polynomial in n, with constants and exponent allowed to depend on G and β?

**Context.** Origin for question 2999: Joint-paper conjecture; Gaudio’s MIT PhD (2020), advisors David Gamarnik and Patrick Jaillet. Setup for question 2999: Fix a finite connected simple graph G and β<0. There are n indistinguishable particles, with occupancy x. At each discrete step choose one particle uniformly, located at i, then move it to j∈{i}∪N(i) with probability proportional to exp[(β/n)(x(j)−1_{j=i})]. Let t_mix be worst-start total-variation mixing time to tolerance 1/4.

**Source.** Julia Gaudio; Yury Polyanskiy. *Attracting random walks*. 2020. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/b77e36ab-ee95-4435-af3b-ad3b51522dfa/content) Location: Conjecture 4.1, journal p. 22; model §2.1, pp. 2–3.

## Q3000. A specified large boundary local limit

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

For θ∈[0,1/2), g_n/n→θ, Σ\_i p_{n,i}=o(n), and p_{n,1}→∞, does T_{n,g_n,p_n}, rooted at the distinguished edge of its first boundary, converge locally in distribution to H_θ?

**Context.** Origin for question 3000: Author’s question; ENS Lyon PhD student, supervised by Thomas Budzinski and Grégory Miermont. Setup for question 3000: Let T_{n,g,p} be uniform among type-I maps of genus g with n+2−2g vertices, labelled simple vertex-disjoint boundary faces of perimeters p=(p₁,…,p_ℓ), and all other faces triangular. Every boundary has a distinguished oriented edge with that face to its right. Define H_θ as the known local limit when g=⌊θn⌋, there is one boundary of length ⌊√n⌋, and the root is its distinguished edge.

**Source.** Tanguy Lions. *Local limits of uniform triangulations with boundaries in high genus*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05491) Location: Paragraph immediately after Theorem 1.1, p. 5.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3000, checked 7 October 2026: March 2026 paper states this extension explicitly; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://sites.google.com/view/tanguylions/)

## Q3092. Sparse combinatorial-matrix least-singular-value upper bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.5) · **Collection** 31

For every ε,δ>0, does some C=C(ε,δ) satisfy P(s_n(M)>C√d/n)≤δ for every n,d with min{d,n−d}≥(1+ε)log n?

**Context.** Origin for question 3092: Authors’ sparse-regime conjecture, distinct from the proved dense case. Setup for question 3092: For integers 0≤d≤n, let M be an n×n 0–1 matrix whose rows are independent and uniform among vectors with exactly d ones. Let s_n(M) denote its least singular value.

**Source.** Dongbin Li; Alexander E. Litvak; Tingzhou Yu. *An upper bound on the smallest singular value of dense random combinatorial matrices*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jco.2026.102031) Location: Conjecture 1.5, pp. 4–5; definition of M_{n,d}.

<a id="q3093"></a>

## Q3093. Independent exponential gaps in the high-temperature Bessel limit

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.4) · **Collection** 31

If independent g_j have exponential rates j(j+a)/2, is (m_k)\_{k≥1} equal in joint law to (∑\_{j≥k}g_j)\_{k≥1}?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3093: Hugo Magaldi. Étude des beta-ensembles et leur limite à haute température. PhD, Université Paris Dauphine–PSL, 2022. Advisors: Djalil Chafaï. Origin for question 3093: Paper-origin conjecture, reached through Magaldi’s doctoral work. Setup for question 3093: Fix a≥0 and one standard Brownian motion B. For each u>0 start at 0 and alternately run its reflected increments with drifts −a/4,(a+1)/4, restarting at 0 whenever u+t/4 is hit. Time t is never reset; all u share B. Let N_u count completed pairs of phases, and define M_a([u,∞))=N_u, with decreasing atoms m_k.

**Source.** Laure Dumaz; Hugo Magaldi. *The spectrum of the stochastic Bessel operator at high temperature*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27602v1) Location: Conjecture 1.4, p. 8; coupled construction pp. 4–6.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3093, checked 8 October 2026: March 2026 preprint retains this joint-law conjecture.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.ceremade.dauphine.fr/fr/actualites/detail-de-lactualite/article/soutenance-de-these-hugo-magaldi-mardi-13-dec.html)


<a id="q3100"></a>

## Q3100. Positive finite prediction coefficients for fractional Gaussian noise

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3) · **Collection** 31

Is Γ\_n^k>0 for every n≥2 and 2≤k≤n?

**Context.** Origin for question 3100: Older Mishura–Ralchenko–Shklyar question from Risks8(2020),11, explicitly retained here. Setup for question 3100: Let B^H be standard fractional Brownian motion with H∈(1/2,1), so E[B_s^HB_t^H]=(s^{2H}+t^{2H}−|t−s|^{2H})/2. Put Δ\_k=B_k^H−B_{k−1}^H. For n≥2 define the unique coefficients Γ\_n^k by E[Δ₁|Δ₂,…,Δ\_n]=∑\_{k=2}^nΓ\_n^kΔ\_k.

**Source.** Iryna Bodnarchuk; Yuliya Mishura; Kostiantyn Ralchenko. *Fractional Gaussian Noise: Projections, Prediction, Norms*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.3390/fractalfract9070428) Location: Conjecture 3, p. 3; definitions (1),(4),(5), pp. 1–2; unresolved-status statement §2, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3100, checked 8 October 2026: Final June 2025 article retains the full conjecture; bounded later-source and correction searches found no resolution.


<a id="q3184"></a>

## Q3184. Strict successive polymer moment thresholds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.1.2) · **Collection** 32

Is β\_{N+1}<β\_N for every integer N≥2?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3184, 3185, 3186: Thesis Conjectures 3.1.2, 3.1.7 and Remark 3.2.8; the first and third also appear in the author’s 2024 paper. Setup for questions 3184, 3185, 3186: Fix d≥3 and a smooth, compactly supported, nonnegative, radial nonincreasing probability density φ; put R=φ\*φ. For space-time white noise W and independent standard Brownian motion B from 0, set Z_T^β=E_B exp{β∫₀ᵀ∫φ(B_s−y)W(ds,dy)−β²R(0)T/2}. Write β\_q=sup{β>0:sup_T E[(Z_T^β)^q]<∞}, γ\*(β)=sup{q>1:sup_T E[(Z_T^β)^q]<∞}, and β\_c=sup{β:P(Z_∞^β>0)=1}, where Z_∞^β=lim_T Z_T^β.

**Source.** Te-Chun Wang. *Renormalized limits of the stochastic heat equation and the Kardar–Parisi–Zhang equation in high dimensions (d≥3)*. 2025. Advisor(s): Yu-Ting Chen, co-supervisor Anthony Quas. [primary source](https://dspace.library.uvic.ca/bitstreams/f1d8e7a3-744e-4baa-bf3a-2d507e057b82/download) Location: Conjecture 3.1.2, p. 53; setup pp. 49–51, 60–61.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3184, 3185, 3186, checked 8 October 2026: Checked 8 October 2026; no later resolution located.
