<a id="q3187"></a>

## Q3187. Worst-case convergence rate of best-edge dynamics

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

Determine the asymptotic order of T_n as n→∞.

**Context.** Origin for question 3187: Thesis §6.6, p. 121; inherited from Chatterjee–Svoboda–Žikelić–Pavlogiannis–Tkadlec, Physical Review E 106 (2022), §VI. Setup for question 3187: Sign every edge of K_n by ±1. A triangle is imbalanced when its sign product is −1; an edge’s rank is the number of imbalanced triangles containing it. At each step choose an imbalanced triangle uniformly, then choose uniformly among its highest-rank edges and flip that sign. Let τ be the first balanced time and T_n the maximum of Eτ over initial signings.

**Source.** Jakub Svoboda. *Structural Properties of Games on Graphs*. 2025. Advisor(s): Krishnendu Chatterjee. [primary source](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/download/20138/20177/2025_Svoboda_Jakub_Thesis.pdf) Location: Definition and Theorem 6, §6.4, p. 113; concluding §6.6, p. 121.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3187, checked 8 October 2026: Finite expected convergence is proved; the general rate remains unquantified in the final paper and thesis. No later resolution located by 8 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://cs.au.dk/~pavlogiannis/publications/papers/pre22.pdf) · [2](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/record/20138)


<a id="q3192"></a>

## Q3192. Hyperuniform stationary activated random walk on intervals

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

Is Var_{π\_n}(|σ|)=o(n), where |σ| counts particles?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3192, 3193: Hyperuniformity recalls Levine–Silvestri (2024), Conjecture 26; the second question is the authors’ Problem 5.6. Setup for questions 3192, 3193: Fix λ>0. On {1,…,n}, active particles jump to either neighbour at total rate 1, exiting particles disappear, lone particles sleep at rate λ, and arrivals awaken sleepers. Each driven step adds one active particle at an independent uniform site and stabilizes. Let π\_n be the stationary law and ρ\_c the fixation threshold on Z for i.i.d. Poisson initial density. A deterministic stable sequence σⁿ is ρ\_c-regular if, for every ε,β>0, eventually every interval of length ≥βn has particle density ≥ρ\_c−ε.

**Source.** Tobias Johnson, Matthew Junge and Josh Meisel. *Stationary activated random walk: regularity, heavy tails, and remixing*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31843v1) Location: §1.1, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3192, 3193, checked 8 October 2026: Current September 2026 version retains both questions. No later resolution located by 8 October 2026.

## Q3194. Diameter-scale robustness of maximal hitting time

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.16) · **Collection** 32

Do universal constants c∈(0,1), C<∞ exist such that t_hit⁺(c/diam G)≤C t_hit for every G?

**Context.** Origin for question 3194: Conjecture 1.16, pp. 118:9–10; definitions 1.8–1.9, p. 118:6. Setup for question 3194: Let P be the simple-random-walk matrix of a finite connected undirected graph G with ≥2 vertices. A history-dependent adversary chooses each transition matrix Q_t subject to (1−ε)P≤Q_t≤(1+ε)P entrywise. For H_v=inf{t≥1:X_t=v}, put t_hit=max_{u≠v}E_u^P H_v and t_hit⁺(ε)=max_{u,v}sup_adversaries E_u H_v.

**Source.** David Zuckerman. *Markov Chain Robustness*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ITCS.2026.118) Location: Conjecture 1.16; §2.1 specifies ordinary, not lazy, random walk.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3194, checked 8 October 2026: Final ITCS version and author publication list checked; no later resolution located by 8 October 2026.

## Q3195. Corner paths as the most likely exponential-LPP geodesics

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

Is P(Γ\_n=c_n)≥P(Γ\_n=γ) for every n≥1 and every up-right path γ?

**Context.** Origin for question 3195: §8, “Most Likely Paths,” pp. 242–243; revisited by McKeown–Nakajima (2026). Setup for question 3195: Give vertices of {0,…,n}² independent Exp(1) weights. Among all up-right paths from (0,0) to (n,n), let Γ\_n be the almost surely unique maximizer of the sum of visited weights. Let c_n follow (0,0)→(0,n)→(n,n).

**Source.** Tom Alberts and Eric Cator. *On the passage time geometry of the last passage percolation problem*. 2021. [primary source](https://par.nsf.gov/servlets/purl/10250710) Location: Alberts–Cator §8, pp. 242–243; McKeown–Nakajima introduction pp. 1–2.

<a id="q3196"></a>

## Q3196. Infinite Schnyder Woods

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.5) · **Collection** 32

Does M(T_n^m) converge locally in distribution to M(H), first n→∞ and then m→∞, preserving edge colours and orientations?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3196: Lukas Michel. PhD, 2026, University of Oxford. Advisors: Alex Scott. Origin for question 3196: Joint-paper Conjecture 1.5; doctoral connection, not asserted thesis-origin. Setup for question 3196: Let T_n^m be uniform rooted simple 2-connected triangulations of an (m+2)-gon with n interior vertices, root r→y and exterior on its right; H is their uncoloured local limit, first n→∞, then m→∞. A wood orients/colours edges B,R,Y. Interior vertices have anticlockwise cyclic pattern B_out,Y_in\*,R_out,B_in\*,Y_out,R_in\*; other boundary vertices have Y_in\*,R_out,B_in\*,Y_out,R_in\* anticlockwise from the exterior. Here \* allows any number of edges. Vertex r has one outgoing edge and only R-incoming edges; y has no outgoing edges and only Y-incoming edges. Maximality excludes anticlockwise cycles and bi-infinite directed paths with the boundary on their right. Write M(T) for the unique maximal wood.

**Source.** Louigi Addario-Berry, Emma Hogan, Lukas Michel and Alex Scott. *Infinite Schnyder Woods*. 2026. [primary source](https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/scott/Papers/schnyder.pdf) Location: Conjecture 1.5, p. 6; §§2–3.1, pp. 8–10,19–20; §6.4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3196, checked 8 October 2026: Current author version retains Conjecture 1.5; no later resolution located by 8 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07601) · [2](https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/michel/)


<a id="q3197"></a>

## Q3197. Monotonicity of the scaled gamma-median offset

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

Is A nonincreasing on (0,∞)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3197, 3198: The author’s 2021 conjectures, explicitly retained after the 2023 proofs of weaker constant bounds. Setup for questions 3197, 3198: For k>0, let ν(k) be the unique solution of ∫₀^{ν(k)}x^{k−1}e^{−x}dx=Γ(k)/2, and put A(k)=2^{1/k}ν(k)−k. Set U=e^{−γ}, L=log2−1/3, R_b(k)=(Lk+Ub)/(k+b), b_L=[8/405−(log2)/3+(log2)²/2]/(U−L), and b_U=(U−L)/(1−Uπ²/12), where γ is Euler’s constant.

**Source.** Richard F. Lyon. *Tight bounds for the median of a gamma distribution*. 2023. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0288601) Location: “Theorems to prove,” p. 2; Conclusions, p. 18.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3197, 3198, checked 8 October 2026: Final article checked. Gaunt–Ouimet2026 resolves different gamma-sum/mixture conjectures. No resolution located by 8 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20212)

<a id="q3199"></a>

## Q3199. Almost-sure critical asymptotics along the full complex phase

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.4) · **Collection** 32

Almost surely, does W_∞(z)/(1−z)→2Z_∞ as z→1 through arbitrary points of P?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3199, 3200: Original paper conjectures; no thesis-origin claim. Setup for questions 3199, 3200: Start one particle at 0. Independently, particles move by standard Brownian motion with drift1, live Exp(r) times and produce L≥0 integer offspring at death, with E[L]>1, E[L²]<∞ and r=1/[2(E[L]−1)]. For alive particles u∈N_t at positions X_u(t), set W_t(z)=∑\_{u∈N_t}exp(−zX_u(t)−(1−z)²t/2), W_∞(z)=lim_tW_t(z), Z_∞=lim_t∑\_{u∈N_t}X_u(t)e^{−X_u(t)}. For z=β+iτ, put P₁={|β|≤1/2,β²+τ²<1/2}∪{1/2<|β|<1,|τ|<1−|β|}, P₁₂={1/2<|β|<1,|τ|=1−|β|}, and P=P₁∪P₁₂.

**Source.** Xinxin Chen and Michel Pain. *Fluctuations of additive martingale limits of branching Brownian motion*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10530v1) Location: Conjecture 1.4, p. 5; model pp. 1–4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3199, 3200, checked 8 October 2026: Latest v1 checked. Non-tangential almost-sure and unrestricted in-probability limits are proved; these stronger statements remain conjectural. No later resolution located by 8 October 2026.

## Q3284. Unimodality of remaining-record dispersion

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 33

For every d, is A_d strictly decreasing up to some a_d∈(0,1.28), then strictly increasing, with a_d decreasing in d?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3284, 3285, 3286, 3287: Thesis Remarks C.4(b) and D.6. Setup for questions 3284, 3285, 3286, 3287: For d≥2 and a>0, let X₁,X₂,… be iid first-d-coordinate projections of Dirichlet(1,…,1,a). A point dominates another if all coordinates are larger. Let r_n count undominated points among X₁,…,X_n, and R_n count indices i≤n whose X_i is undominated by earlier points. Set A_d(a)=lim_n Var(r_n)/E r_n, B_d(a)=lim_n Var(R_n)/E R_n, and A_d(∞)=lim_{a→∞}A_d(a); these limits exist.

**Source.** Ao Sun. *Studies in Multivariate Pareto Records*. 2025. Advisor(s): James Allen Fill. [primary source](https://jscholarship.library.jhu.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/7ca48c8a-5406-49b2-979f-adf49c725f4d/content) Location: Remark C.4(b), p. 243.

**Source.** Ao Sun. *Studies in Multivariate Pareto Records*. 2025. Advisor(s): James Allen Fill. [primary source](https://jscholarship.library.jhu.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/7ca48c8a-5406-49b2-979f-adf49c725f4d/content) Location: Remark D.6, p. 263.

**Further links.** [1](https://jscholarship.library.jhu.edu/items/3c4d15c0-ee1d-43c7-94ef-dfb997ae860f)

**Result (2026-10-09).** For every fixed integer d >= 2, B_d(a) has a finite limit C_d > 1 as a tends to infinity. A convergent positive double-integral formula gives C_2 = 1 + pi^2/3 and C_3 = 1 + 12 zeta(3). The n-to-infinity limit defining B_d is taken first. No mathematical gap identified relative to the fixed-parameter formula in Sun, Theorem 4.8 and Remark 4.10. The proof concerns record arrivals R_n, not remaining records r_n; external peer review is still needed.

[Proof](../../solutions/seven-problems-2026-10-09/analysis/REPORT.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/seven-problems-2026-10-09/reviews/analysis-review.md) · [Computational controls](../../solutions/seven-problems-2026-10-09/analysis/checks.json). This research proposal has a separate internal AI review; it is not externally peer reviewed.


<a id="q3287"></a>

## Q3287. Small-parameter record-count dispersion

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 33

Is lim_{a↓0}B_d(a)=1 for every d≥2?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3284, 3285, 3286, 3287: Thesis Remarks C.4(b) and D.6. Setup for questions 3284, 3285, 3286, 3287: For d≥2 and a>0, let X₁,X₂,… be iid first-d-coordinate projections of Dirichlet(1,…,1,a). A point dominates another if all coordinates are larger. Let r_n count undominated points among X₁,…,X_n, and R_n count indices i≤n whose X_i is undominated by earlier points. Set A_d(a)=lim_n Var(r_n)/E r_n, B_d(a)=lim_n Var(R_n)/E R_n, and A_d(∞)=lim_{a→∞}A_d(a); these limits exist.

**Source.** Ao Sun. *Studies in Multivariate Pareto Records*. 2025. Advisor(s): James Allen Fill. [primary source](https://jscholarship.library.jhu.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/7ca48c8a-5406-49b2-979f-adf49c725f4d/content) Location: Remark D.6, p. 263.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3284, 3285, 3286, 3287, checked 8 October 2026: Checked 8 October 2026; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://jscholarship.library.jhu.edu/items/3c4d15c0-ee1d-43c7-94ef-dfb997ae860f)

**Result (2026-10-09).** For every fixed integer d >= 2, B_d(a) = 1 - 2a/(d-1) + O_d(a^2) as a decreases to zero. In particular its limit is one, with underdispersion for all sufficiently small positive a. The n-to-infinity limit defining B_d is taken first. No mathematical gap identified relative to the fixed-parameter formula in Sun, Theorem 4.8 and Remark 4.10. The proof concerns record arrivals R_n, not remaining records r_n; external peer review is still needed.

[Proof](../../solutions/seven-problems-2026-10-09/analysis/REPORT.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/seven-problems-2026-10-09/reviews/analysis-review.md) · [Computational controls](../../solutions/seven-problems-2026-10-09/analysis/checks.json). This research proposal has a separate internal AI review; it is not externally peer reviewed.

## Q3288. Finite-space distribution-map convergence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5) · **Collection** 33

For every finite S with pairwise distinct nonzero distances from each point, do πⁿ\_{j,k}(θ) converge for almost every θ to a point mass or a uniform two-point law?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3288, 3289, 3290: Conjectures 5, 7 and §7.3, second question. Setup for questions 3288, 3289, 3290: For compact metric S, k≥2 and 1≤j≤k, a chain at x samples k independent points from θ and jumps to the jth nearest, breaking ties uniformly. Let π\_{j,k}(θ) be its unique stationary law; πⁿ denotes iteration of this distribution map. On finite S, almost every means Lebesgue measure on the probability simplex.

**Source.** David J. Aldous, Madelyn Cruz and Shi Feng. *Markov chains and mappings of distributions on compact spaces II: Numerics and Conjectures*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18153v1) Location: Conjecture 5, pp. 14–15.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3288, 3289, 3290, checked 8 October 2026: Checked 8 October 2026; no later resolution located.

<a id="q3291"></a>

## Q3291. Finitary coding of union fields

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 33

Characterize the families (p_A) for which X̄ is ffiid.

**Context.** Origin for questions 3291, 3292: §1, Open problems 1–2. Setup for questions 3291, 3292: Let d≥1. Independently select every finite nonempty A⊂Z^d with probability p_A∈[0,1), where p_{A+v}=p_A and Σ\_{A∋0}p_A<∞. Set X̄\_v=1 if a selected set contains v, and X̂\_v={finite A⊂Z^d:0∈A, A+v is selected}. A process is ffiid if it is the distributional image of an iid process under a translation-equivariant measurable map whose value at 0 is determined by an almost-surely finite random neighborhood.

**Source.** Yinon Spinka. *Finitary codings and stochastic domination for Poisson representable processes*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04797v1) Location: §1, Open problem 1, p. 6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3291, 3292, checked 8 October 2026: Checked 8 October 2026; no later resolution located.

## Q3295. Binary-tree game convergence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.1) · **Collection** 33

For d=2, does V_n converge in distribution as n→∞?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3295, 3296: §6, Problems 6.1, 6.4 and 6.8. Setup for questions 3295, 3296: Assign independent uniform ±1 weights to the edges of one infinite rooted d-ary tree. Let V_n be the resulting game value; all n use this same weighted tree.

## Q3436. Real-rooted finite commutators

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.3) · **Collection** 35

For every n≥1 and Hermitian A,B, does E[det(xI−C)] have only real zeros?

**Context.** For Hermitian n×n matrices A,B and Haar-uniform U∈U(n), put C=i(AUBU\*−UBU\*A). Separately, write p(x)=Σ\_{k=0}^m(−1)^k e_k(p)x^{m−k}, and (a)\_k=a(a−1)⋯(a−k+1), (a)\_0=1. Define p⊞ᵅ\_m q by e_k(p⊞ᵅ\_m q)=(m)\_k(m+α)\_k Σ\_{i+j=k} e_i(p)e_j(q)/[(m)\_i(m+α)\_i(m)\_j(m+α)\_j]. Origin: Commutator, Campbell thesis Question 4.1; rectangular conjecture, Gribinski’s 2023 question in the later joint paper. https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31356v1 https://dspacemainprd01.lib.uwaterloo.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/aac8c03b-e3b1-470b-b3d7-e8a1018b45d2/content https://uwaterloo.ca/pure-mathematics/events/phd-thesis-defence-30

**Source.** Jacob Campbell, Rafael Morales and Daniel Perales. *Even Hypergeometric Polynomials and Finite Free Commutators*. 2025. [primary source](https://sigma-journal.com/2025/108/sigma25-108.pdf) Location: Theorem 5.2 and Conjecture 5.3, p. 20.

**Literature check.** Status: Checked 9 October 2026; no later resolution located. Rectangular α=−1/2 and nonnegative integers are proved.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31356v1) · [2](https://dspacemainprd01.lib.uwaterloo.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/aac8c03b-e3b1-470b-b3d7-e8a1018b45d2/content) · [3](https://uwaterloo.ca/pure-mathematics/events/phd-thesis-defence-30)

<a id="q3438"></a>

## Q3438. Integrable initial opinions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 35

For every iid initial field with E|f₀(v)|<∞, does f_t(v)→μ almost surely for every v?

**Context.** On an infinite connected simple bounded-degree graph G, independent rate-one Poisson clocks ring on edges. Each ring replaces both endpoint opinions by their arithmetic mean. The initial real-valued field is independent of the clocks; μ=E[f₀(v)]. Gantert–Vilkas posed the first question; the second is this paper’s. Discovery: Hirscher/Vilkas’s doctoral branch. https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08783 https://publications.lib.chalmers.se/records/fulltext/234563/234563.pdf https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/234563 Doctoral context: Timo Hirscher (now Timo Vilkas), Chalmers PhD 2016; advisor Olle Häggström.

**Source.** Dor Elboim, Yuval Peres and Ron Peretz. *The edge-averaging process on graphs with random initial opinions*. 2025. [primary source](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12377646/) Location: §7, Problem 1.

**Literature check.** Status: Checked 9 October 2026. May 2026 moment-dependence paper still leaves weaker-moment almost-sure convergence open.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08783) · [2](https://publications.lib.chalmers.se/records/fulltext/234563/234563.pdf) · [3](https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/234563)

**Context.** On an infinite connected simple bounded-degree graph G, independent rate-one Poisson clocks ring on edges. Each ring replaces both endpoint opinions by their arithmetic mean. <a id="q3440"></a>

## Q3440. Markovian segregation above 1/e

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

Do P,x,y and a Markovian coupling exist with P(τ<∞)=1 and lim_{n→∞}||P^n(x,·)−P^n(y,·)||TV>1/e?

**Context.** Let P be a transition matrix on a finite set S. A coupling has marginals X,Y that individually are time-homogeneous P-Markov chains started at x,y. It is Markovian if (X_n,Y_n) itself is a Markov chain, possibly time-inhomogeneous; it need not be faithful. Put τ=inf{n≥0:X_n=Y_n} and ||μ−ν||TV=½Σ\_s|μ(s)−ν(s)|. Joint-paper Remark 3; Martinsson thesis Paper II is the doctoral discovery route. https://publications.lib.chalmers.se/records/fulltext/248704/248704.pdf https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/248704

**Source.** Timo Hirscher and Anders Martinsson. *Segregating Markov Chains*. 2018. [primary source](https://research.chalmers.se/publication/504642/file/504642_Fulltext.pdf) Location: §6, Remark 3, p. 1536.

**Literature check.** Status: Checked 9 October 2026; no later improvement located. The unrestricted-coupling supremum 1/2 does not answer this restriction.

**Further links.** [1](https://publications.lib.chalmers.se/records/fulltext/248704/248704.pdf) · [2](https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/248704)


<a id="q3441"></a>

## Q3441. Critical uniform Deffuant model

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

For every μ∈(0,1/2], does η\_t(v)→1/2 almost surely for every v∈Z?

**Context.** On Z, start iid η₀(v) uniform on [0,1]. Each nearest-neighbor edge has an independent rate-one Poisson clock, independent of initial opinions. Fix μ∈(0,1/2]. At a ring, endpoint values a,b become ((1−μ)a+μb,μa+(1−μ)b) if |a−b|≤1/2, and otherwise remain unchanged. Critical case after Theorem 2.1, already posed by earlier Lanchier/Häggström work; not thesis-origin. https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01593 https://publications.lib.chalmers.se/records/fulltext/234563/234563.pdf https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/234563

**Source.** Olle Häggström and Timo Hirscher. *Further results on consensus formation in the Deffuant model*. 2014. [primary source](https://gup.ub.gu.se/file/192663) Location: After Theorem 2.1, p. 4; discussion after Proposition 2.4, p. 11.

**Literature check.** Status: Checked 9 October 2026; no matching critical-case resolution located. Finite heterogeneous-model convergence uses different hypotheses.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01593) · [2](https://publications.lib.chalmers.se/records/fulltext/234563/234563.pdf) · [3](https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/234563)


<a id="q3442"></a>

## Q3442. Sharp jig-type constant

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

For every ε>0, when q≥(2/√e+ε)n, are all solutions similar with probability tending to 1 as n→∞, for every choice of ι?

**Context.** Fix an involution ι on [q]. Uniformly choose a compatible assignment of jig types to all sides of an n×n grid of square pieces: opposite sides along each shared edge receive a uniform type j and its complement ι(j); different grid edges, including boundary edges, are independent. A solution reassembles the pieces into an n×n square with compatible internal sides, permitting rotations but no flips. Solutions are similar when their jig assignments agree up to global rotation. Thesis §5.2, p. 35; final article’s conjecture after Theorem 1.1, p. 289. https://publications.lib.chalmers.se/records/fulltext/248704/248704.pdf https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/248704

**Source.** Anders Martinsson. *A Linear Threshold for Uniqueness of Solutions to Random Jigsaw Puzzles*. 2019. [primary source](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/A7B4B084923555FA362C457AD4E25CB7/S0963548318000391a.pdf/div-class-title-a-linear-threshold-for-uniqueness-of-solutions-to-random-jigsaw-puzzles-div.pdf) Location: §1, after Theorem 1.1, p. 289.

**Literature check.** Status: Checked 9 October 2026; no matching sharp-constant result located. Unique edge assembly allows duplicate pieces.

**Further links.** [1](https://publications.lib.chalmers.se/records/fulltext/248704/248704.pdf) · [2](https://research.chalmers.se/en/publication/248704)


<a id="q3485"></a>

## Q3485. Weak-coupling limit from empty environment

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

For every T>0, does εX^{(ε)}\_{t/ε²} converge in law on C([0,T],R²) to (1+4πα²)^(1/4)W_t as ε↓0, where W is standard planar Brownian motion?

**Context.** Giles: Fluctuations, diffusivity, and superdiffusivity in statistical mechanics, Warwick PhD thesis 2025; advisor Giuseppe Cannizzaro. Origin: Paper’s stated extension; thesis is embargoed, so no thesis-origin claim. Setup: Let V:R²→R be smooth, radial, positive-definite, rapidly decaying, with ∫V=1. For α>0, γ\_ε=α/√log(1+ε⁻²), let X^{(ε)}\_0=0 and dX_t=dB_t−γ\_ε²[∫₀ᵗ∇V(X_t−X_s)ds]dt, where B is standard planar Brownian motion. This is zero initial environment.

**Source.** Giuseppe Cannizzaro and Harry Giles. *An invariance principle for the 2d weakly self-repelling Brownian polymer*. 2026. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1364545241/34) Location: §1.2, zero-environment remark and Theorem 1.2.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Final 2026 article explicitly predicts the zero-environment extension. Later fixed/weak-coupling and correction searches found no proof.


<a id="q3585"></a>

## Q3585. Optimal anonymous channel capture

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

Is z\*\_n=z_n for every positive integer n?

**Context.** Degree: PhD Advisors: Eytan Modiano. Source type: Paper after dissertation-first search Origin: Neely, Repeated Games, Optimal Channel Capture, and Open Problems for Slotted Multiple Access; expanded arXiv:2110.09638v3. Setup: There are n anonymous users. Each uses the same randomized rule with independent private randomness, knows n, and observes its own transmissions and the number of transmitters after every slot. A success means exactly one transmitter. Let z\*\_n be the infimum expected first-success time over these rules. Set z_1=1, and recursively for n≥2 set z_n=inf_{0&lt;p<1} [1+Σ\_{i=2}^{n−1} min(z_i,z_{n−i}) binom(n,i)p^i(1−p)^{n−i}]/[1−p^n−(1−p)^n].

**Source.** Michael J. Neely. *Repeated Games, Optimal Channel Capture, and Open Problems for Slotted Multiple Access*. MIT, 2022. [primary source](https://allerton.csl.illinois.edu/files/2022/12/2022-115-paper_4748.pdf) Location: Conference paper §V, p.7, with recurrence (16); full preprint §V, p.12.

**Literature check.** Status: The 2022 conference version reaffirms the conjecture; n=1,2,3,4,6 are proved. No later resolution located, checked 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/18af1639-f17d-48d7-b039-1b23b31ceffa)

**Source.** Michael J. Neely. *Repeated Games, Optimal Channel Capture, and Open Problems for Slotted Multiple Access*. MIT, 2022. <a id="q3587"></a>

## Q3587. Bulk gaps of Lévy random walks

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

For fixed 0<α<1, determine the asymptotic law, including the appropriate scaling, of Δ\_{⌊αn⌋,n} as n→∞.

**Context.** Degree: doctorate Advisors: Grégory Schehr, Satya N. Majumdar. Source type: PhD dissertation Origin: Thesis conclusion; the bulk-gap question extends edge-gap results, while the Brownian-area question also appears in the earlier confined-Brownian-motion paper. Setup: For the bulk-gap question, let S_j=η\_1+⋯+η\_j, where the increments are iid with a continuous symmetric density f(x)∼c|x|^{−1−μ}, c>0, 0<μ<2. Arrange S_1,…,S_n as M_{1,n}>⋯>M_{n,n}, and put Δ\_{k,n}=M_{k,n}−M_{k+1,n}. For the Brownian-area question, let B_t be standard planar Brownian motion normally reflected in the unit disk, started at its center, and A_t the area of conv{B_s:0≤s≤t}.

**Source.** Benjamin De Bruyne. *Extreme value statistics and optimization problems in stochastic processes*. Université Paris-Saclay, 2023. Advisor(s): Grégory Schehr, Satya N. Majumdar. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.14779) Location: Thesis conclusion, printed p.99.

**Literature check.** Status: No later resolution located, checked 2026-10-09. The 2026 stopped-at-exit area result concerns a different process; the 2025 fragmentation paper treats perimeter.

## Q3591. Planar favorite-point fluctuations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 36

Do some c>0 and almost surely finite positive random variable Z satisfy lim_{N→∞}Pr(M_N≤2π^{−1/2}log N−π^{−1/2}log log N+u)=E[exp(−Ze^{−cu})] for every u∈ℝ?

**Context.** Degree: PhD Advisors: Nathanaël Berestycki. Source type: Dissertation-led paper Origin: Jego, Critical Brownian multiplicative chaos, PTRF 180 (2021), 495–552. This is the maximum-law part of Conjecture 1. Setup: A discrete-time simple random walk starts at 0∈ℤ² and stops on leaving [−N,N]². Write ℓ\_N(x) for its number of visits to x and M_N=max_x√ℓ\_N(x).

**Source.** Antoine Jego. *Critical Brownian multiplicative chaos*. University of Cambridge, 2021. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1241735298/34) Location: Published paper §1.2, Conjecture 1, p.500; compare Biskup (2025), §5.3.

**Literature check.** Status: The maximum-law problem is reaffirmed in Biskup’s 2025 notes. No resolution located by 2026-10-09; the 2026 wired cover-time theorem addresses a different statistic.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/items/0ebf3e37-48cd-4720-bb1a-443cdee93fc6)


<a id="q3592"></a>

## Q3592. A simultaneous Ray–Knight coupling

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.15) · **Collection** 36

Can L be coupled with a càdlàg process h(t), each marginal distributed as h, so that {h(s):s≤t} is independent of {L_{t+u}−L_t:u≥0}, and ½(h(t)+√(2t))²−L_t is almost surely constant in t≥0, coordinatewise?

**Context.** Degree: PhD Advisors: Frank den Hollander, Roman Kotecký. Source type: Dissertation-led lecture notes Origin: Biskup, Extremal properties of the random walk local time (2025), Question 2.15. Setup: Let X be a rate-one finite irreducible reversible continuous-time chain on V∪{ρ}, started at ρ, with reversible measure π. Set ℓ\_s(x)=π(x)^{−1}∫₀ˢ1_{X_u=x}du, τ(t)=inf{s:ℓ\_s(ρ)>t}, and L_t(x)=ℓ\_{τ(t)}(x). Let h be centered Gaussian with h(ρ)=0 and covariance G(x,y)=E_x[ℓ\_{T_ρ}(y)], where T_ρ is the first hit of ρ.

**Source.** Marek Biskup. *Extremal properties of the random walk local time*. Katholieke Universiteit Nijmegen, 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09853) Location: Question 2.15, printed p.19 / PDF p.20.

**Literature check.** Status: No later solution located, checked 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~biskup/PDFs/papers/thesis.pdf)


<a id="q3593"></a>

## Q3593. Removing dimensional moment restrictions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 8.1) · **Collection** 36

For positive nearest-neighbor c that is averaging-ergodic, does an invariance principle follow from sup_R |Λ\_R|^{−1}Σ\_{e∈E_R}[c(e)^p+c(e)^{−q}]<∞ for any p,q>1?

**Context.** Degree: PhD Advisors: Frank den Hollander, Roman Kotecký. Source type: Dissertation-led later paper Origin: Homogenization theory of random walks among deterministic conductances, PTRF 194 (2026), 41–102. Setup: Let Λ\_R=[−R,R]^d∩ℤ^d and E_R be its incident nearest-neighbor edges. A symmetric conductance configuration c with c(x,x)=0 is averaging-ergodic when |Λ\_R|^{−1}Σ\_{x∈Λ\_R}δ\_{τ\_xc} converges weakly to a translation-ergodic probability law. The walk starts at 0 with transition probability c(x,y)/Σ\_zc(x,z). An invariance principle means its diffusively rescaled, linearly interpolated paths converge in law to nondegenerate centered Brownian motion.

**Source.** Marek Biskup. *Homogenization theory of random walks among deterministic conductances*. Katholieke Universiteit Nijmegen, 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.08382v4) Location: Conjecture 8.1, accepted manuscript p.45.

**Literature check.** Status: Conjectures remain in the December 2025 accepted manuscript; no later resolution located, checked 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~biskup/PDFs/papers/thesis.pdf)

<a id="q3595"></a>

## Q3595. Coincidence of three-dimensional loop-soup thresholds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.6) · **Collection** 36

Must α\_c=α\_u=α\_tr=α\_B?

**Context.** Degree: PhD Advisors: Nathanaël Berestycki. Source type: Dissertation-led later paper Origin: Jego–Lupu, Three-dimensional Brownian loop soup clusters. Setup: In ℝ³ let ℒ\_α be the Poisson soup with intensity α∫ℝ³dx∫₀∞dt/[t(2πt)^{3/2}] P^{x,x;t}, where P^{x,x;t} is Brownian-bridge law. Clusters are connected components under finite chains of intersecting loops. Define α\_c, α\_u, α\_tr as the infima of intensities giving positive probability of, respectively, an unbounded cluster, exactly one unbounded cluster, and an unbounded cluster using only loops of diameter≥1. Define α\_B as the infimum giving positive probability that B(0,1) connects to every ∂B(0,R), R>1.

**Source.** Antoine Jego and Titus Lupu. *Three-dimensional Brownian loop soup clusters*. University of Cambridge, 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04840v2) Location: Conjecture 1.6, p.4; definitions (1.3)–(1.5), p.3, and (1.10), p.4.

**Literature check.** Status: Conjecture persists in January 2026 v2; no later resolution located, checked 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/items/0ebf3e37-48cd-4720-bb1a-443cdee93fc6)


<a id="q3596"></a>

## Q3596. Persistence of draws under neutralization

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

For every κ≥2 and χ, if d_{i,j}(ρ)>0, p_0>ρ\_0, p_1≤ρ\_1 and p_{−1}≤ρ\_{−1}, must d_{i,j}(p)>0?

**Context.** Degree: PhD Advisors: Joel Spencer. Source type: Genealogy-led later paper Origin: Karmakar–Podder–Roy–Sadhukhan, Percolation games on rooted, edge-weighted random trees. Setup: A rooted Galton–Watson tree with fixed offspring law χ on ℕ₀, χ(0)<1 is revealed to both players. Independently, each edge has weight a∈{−1,0,1} with probability p_a. Starting at the root with capitals i,j∈{1,…,κ−1}, player 1 moves first; players alternate moving to a child and adding that edge’s weight to their own capital. Reaching κ wins; reaching 0 loses; a player with no move loses. Let d_{i,j}(p) be the probability neither player can force a win.

**Source.** Karmakar, Podder, Roy and Sadhukhan. *Percolation games on rooted, edge-weighted random trees*. New York University, 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00831v2) Location: §5, unnumbered phase-transition conjecture, p.17.

**Literature check.** Status: This conjecture persists in January 2025 v2; no later resolution located by 2026-10-09.

## Q3599. A double-edge excursion asymptotic

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

Does n^{1+δ−δ/(3+2δ)}Pr(T_n&lt;R_0) converge to a finite positive constant for every δ>0?

**Context.** Degree: PhD Advisors: Daniel Kious, Hendrik Weber. Source type: PhD dissertation Origin: Thesis Chapter3, conditional Theorem2.1; the unresolved hypothesis is Conjecture7.1, equation(7.2), with discussion in §5.1. Setup: Replace each edge of the half-line {0,1,2,…} by two parallel edges. Starting at 0, a walker chooses an incident edge proportionally to its current weight. Every edge initially has weight1; its first traversal permanently changes that weight to 1+δ, where δ>0. Put T_n=inf{k≥1:X_k=n} and R_0=inf{k≥1:X_k=0}.

**Source.** Pawel Rudnicki. *Random walks with reinforcement*. University of Bath, 2024. Advisor(s): Daniel Kious, Hendrik Weber. [primary source](https://purehost.bath.ac.uk/ws/portalfiles/portal/344232966/Pawel_Rudnicki_-_Thesis.pdf) Location: Printed p.82 / PDF p.95; model printed pp.79–81; future directions p.169.

**Literature check.** Status: The thesis proves this only conditional on its exit-distribution conjecture. No unconditional proof located by 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://researchportal.bath.ac.uk/en/studentTheses/random-walks-with-reinforcement/)

## Q3600. Critical radius for coordinate-wise connections

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

Is R_c^NN(∞)=R_c^dec=1?

**Context.** Degree: doctorate Advisors: Jean Mairesse. Source type: Dissertation-led later paper Origin: Casse–Marcovici–Poutrel, Gilbert’s disc model conditioned on the square lattice. Setup: Choose independent P_z=(X_z,Y_z), uniformly in z+[0,1]², z∈ℤ². G^CN_{p,R} joins z≠w when ‖P_z−P_w‖\_p≤R; G^NN retains only nearest-neighbor lattice edges. G^dec_R keeps horizontal edges z↔z+e_1 when X_{z+e_1}−X_z≤R and vertical edges analogously using Y. For each model, R_c is the infimum of R giving positive probability of an infinite origin cluster.

**Source.** Casse, Marcovici and Poutrel. *Gilbert’s disc model conditioned on the square lattice*. Université Paris Diderot, 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14062v1) Location: §5, Conjecture13, printed p.28.

## Q3690. Is Δ\_{p_c}<∞ for every sufficiently large d?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.48) · **Collection** 37

Is Δ\_{p_c}<∞ for every sufficiently large d?

**Context.** Take intensity-λ Poisson–Voronoi percolation in ℝᵈ (d≥2), black-cell probability p, threshold p_c. Let τ\_p(x,y) be the connectivity probability after inserting x,y as black; Δ\_p=λ²∫\_{ℝᵈ}∫\_{ℝᵈ}τ\_p(0,x)τ\_p(x,y)τ\_p(y,0)dxdy. G(n,α) has n i.i.d. uniform unit-Sᵈ vertices, joined when geodesic distance>π−α. A_n sharply thresholds χ>k if probabilities at (1−ε)A_n and (1+ε)A_n tend to 0 and 1 for each ε∈(0,1). Let c₂=(σ\_dλ\_AB)¹ᐟᵈ, with σ\_d sphere area and λ\_AB the critical equal intensity of two independent Euclidean Poisson processes joined across types at distance≤1.

**Source.** Matthias Irlbeck. *High-Dimensional Poisson–Voronoi Geometry and Threshold Phenomena*. University of Groningen, 2026. Advisor(s): Tobias Müller, Pieter Trapman. [primary source](https://www.matthiasirlbeck.com/documents/PhD_thesis_matthias_irlbeck.pdf) Location: Conjecture 3.48, p.77

**Literature check.** Status: September 2026 thesis and March 2026 v2 inspected; no later resolution located.

## Q3693. Differentiability of the hyperbolic uniqueness threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 25) · **Collection** 37

Is λ↦p_u(λ) differentiable on (0,∞)?

**Context.** Origin: Irlbeck–Müller, Non-vanishing uniqueness threshold for hyperbolic Poisson-Voronoi percolation in dimension at least three. Setup: On hyperbolic space ℍᵈ, d≥3, form the Voronoi tessellation of a homogeneous Poisson process with intensity λ>0 relative to hyperbolic volume. Color cells independently black with probability p. Black clusters are the connected components of their union. Let p_u(λ) be the infimum of p for which exactly one unbounded black cluster exists with positive probability.

**Source.** Irlbeck and Müller. *Non-vanishing uniqueness threshold for hyperbolic Poisson-Voronoi percolation in dimension at least three*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17764v1) Location: Question 25, p.30.

## Q3694. Maximum spread for four-wise independence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.13) · **Collection** 37

Is Msp(n,4)=⌊√(n−2)⌋+2 for every n≥6?

**Context.** Origin: Berend–Ernst–Kontorovich–Kumar, Exact Expressions for the Maximal Probability that all k-wise Independent Bits are 1. Setup: For even 2≤k≤n−1, take I={a₁,a₁+1,…,a_{k/2},a_{k/2}+1,n}⊂{0,…,n}, with distinct increasing entries. For p∈[0,1], let (w_j(p))\_{j∈I} be the unique solution of Σ\_{j∈I}(j)\_r w_j=(n)\_r p^r, 0≤r≤k, where (x)\_r is the falling factorial. Call I realizable if all weights are nonnegative throughout some nondegenerate p-interval. Define Msp(n,k)=max_I(a_{k/2}+1−a₁) over realizable I.

**Source.** Berend, Ernst, Kontorovich and Kumar. *Exact Expressions for the Maximal Probability that all k-wise Independent Bits are 1*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10959-026-01487-4) Location: Conjecture 5.13, p.41 of 45.

**Literature check.** Status: The inspected May2026 version of record retains this conjecture. No later resolution located by 2026-10-09.

<a id="q3695"></a>

## Q3695. Sharp temporal-connectivity radius

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 37

Does some κ=κ(K,d)>0 give temporal-connectivity probability tending to 0 at r_n=(κ−ε)n^(−1/(d+1)) and to 1 at r_n=(κ+ε)n^(−1/(d+1)), for every 0<ε<κ?

**Context.** Origin: Brandenberger–Donderwinkel–Kerriou–Lugosi–Mitchell, Temporal connectivity of random geometric graphs. Setup: Let d≥2 and K:[0,∞)→[0,1] be nonincreasing, with K(x)≥a>0 for x≤1 and K(x)≤b x^(−d)exp(−2(x+1)log(x+1)) for x>1, for constants a,b>0. Sample n independent uniform points on the unit d-torus. Conditionally independently join x,y with probability K(dist(x,y)/r_n), and give edges independent Uniform[0,1] timestamps. Temporal connectivity means every ordered vertex pair has a path with increasing timestamps.

**Source.** Brandenberger, Donderwinkel, Kerriou, Lugosi and Mitchell. *Temporal connectivity of random geometric graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jpr.2026.10129) Location: Thesis §4.4 pp.183–184; final paper §1 p.4.

**Literature check.** Status: September2026 final journal article reaffirms the thesis conjecture; no later resolution located by 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://kups.ub.uni-koeln.de/80375/)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 37

For 1≤j≤min deg, suppose j<Φ\_E+1, or some o satisfies |N(x)∖B_k(o)|≥j whenever dist(o,x)=k. For each p,q∈(q_c,1], does some c>0 give |E_{µ\_p}f(η\_t)−µ\_qf|≤c⁻¹|supp f|‖f‖∞e^(−ct) for every local f and t≥0?

FA-jf refreshes spins from Ber(q) at rate 1 if ≥j neighbours are 1; q_c is its L²(µ\_q) ergodicity threshold.

## Q3782. For each fixed r≥3, does containing a K_r-factor have a sharp threshold as n→∞ through multiples…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 38

For each fixed r≥3, does containing a K_r-factor have a sharp threshold as n→∞ through multiples of r? A K_r-factor partitions vertices into r-cliques.

**Context.** Origin: Joint chapter with Calum MacRury; clique-factor question restates Ben-Eliezer et al. Setup: Start empty on [n]. Each round offers an independent uniform vertex; an online strategy joins it to a distinct vertex. Repeated edges are allowed. Let m_P(θ,n) be the least number of rounds permitting success probability ≥θ. A sharp threshold means m_P(θ,n)/m_P(1/2,n)→1 for every fixed θ∈(0,1).

**Source.** Erlang Surya. *Concentration and Sharp Thresholds in Random Graphs*. University of California San Diego, 2025. Advisor(s): Lutz Warnke. [primary source](https://mathweb.ucsd.edu/~lwarnke/PhD_Thesis_ErlangSurya.pdf) Location: Thesis §4.5, Question 1, p.171.

Each round offers an independent uniform vertex; an online strategy joins it to a distinct vertex. Repeated edges are allowed. Let m_P(θ,n) be the least number of rounds permitting success probability ≥θ. A sharp threshold means m_P(θ,n)/m_P(1/2,n)→1 for every fixed θ∈(0,1).

**Source.** Erlang Surya. *Concentration and Sharp Thresholds in Random Graphs*. University of California San Diego, 2025. Advisor(s): Lutz Warnke. [primary source](https://mathweb.ucsd.edu/~lwarnke/PhD_Thesis_ErlangSurya.pdf) Location: Thesis §4.5, Question 2, p.171; Theorem 7.

**Literature check.** Status: Published chapter reaffirmed in 2025 thesis; fixed-degree constants remain unresolved.

**Further links.** [1](https://mathweb.ucsd.edu/~lwarnke/)

## Q3785. For fixed integer r≥3 and c>0, determine the asymptotic value of χ(G(n,1−c n^{−2/r})) with…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

For fixed integer r≥3 and c>0, determine the asymptotic value of χ(G(n,1−c n^{−2/r})) with probability tending to one.

**Context.** Paper: On the concentration of the chromatic number of random graphs; Erlang Surya, Lutz Warnke. Origin: Explicit final-paper open problem, restricted to its unresolved r≥3 regime. <a id="q3788"></a>

## Q3788. Voronoi mean-field exponents from the triangle condition

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.47) · **Collection** 38

Does Δ\_{p_c}<∞ imply E_p|C₀|≍(p_c−p)⁻¹ for 0≤p&lt;p_c, P_{p_c}(|C₀|≥n)≍n⁻¹ᐟ² for n≥1, and P_p(|C₀|=∞)≍p−p_c as p↓p_c?

**Context.** Origin: Thesis Conjecture 3.47. Setup: In ℝᵈ, d≥2, independently color intensity-λ (λ>0) Poisson–Voronoi cells black with probability p; p_c is the threshold for an unbounded black component. Let C₀ be the set of nuclei in the origin’s black cluster after adding 0 as a black point. Let τ\_p(x,y) be the connection probability after adding x,y and forcing their cells black. Put Δ\_p=λ²∫∫τ\_p(0,x)τ\_p(x,y)τ\_p(y,0)dxdy. Here ≍ denotes two-sided constant-factor bounds.

**Source.** Matthias Irlbeck. *High-Dimensional Poisson–Voronoi Geometry and Threshold Phenomena*. University of Groningen, 2026. Advisor(s): Tobias Müller, Pieter Trapman. [primary source](https://www.matthiasirlbeck.com/documents/PhD_thesis_matthias_irlbeck.pdf) Location: Conjecture 3.47, pp. 76–77; forced-black origin §3.2.2, p. 21.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Full September 2026 thesis inspected. Author currently lists the corresponding paper as forthcoming; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://research.rug.nl/en/publications/high-dimensional-poissonvoronoi-geometry-and-threshold-phenomena/)


<a id="q3789"></a>

## Q3789. Strict decrease of the hyperbolic uniqueness threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 26) · **Collection** 38

Is λ↦p_u(λ) strictly decreasing on (0,∞)?

**Context.** Origin: Irlbeck–Müller, Non-vanishing uniqueness threshold for hyperbolic Poisson-Voronoi percolation in dimension at least three. Setup: On hyperbolic space ℍᵈ, d≥3, form the Voronoi tessellation of a homogeneous Poisson process with intensity λ>0 relative to hyperbolic volume. Color cells independently black with probability p. Black clusters are the connected components of their union. Let p_u(λ) be the infimum of p for which exactly one unbounded black cluster exists with positive probability.

**Source.** Irlbeck and Müller. *Non-vanishing uniqueness threshold for hyperbolic Poisson-Voronoi percolation in dimension at least three*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17764v1) Location: Question 26, p. 30.

<a id="q3790"></a>

## Q3790. Monotonicity of realizable-basis spread

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.12) · **Collection** 38

For every fixed even k, is Msp(n,k) nondecreasing in n≥k+1?

**Context.** Origin: Berend–Ernst–Kontorovich–Kumar, Exact Expressions for the Maximal Probability that all k-wise Independent Bits are 1. Setup: For even 2≤k≤n−1, take I={a₁,a₁+1,…,a_{k/2},a_{k/2}+1,n}⊂{0,…,n}, with distinct increasing entries. For p∈[0,1], let (w_j(p))\_{j∈I} be the unique solution of Σ\_{j∈I}(j)\_r w_j=(n)\_r p^r, 0≤r≤k, where (x)\_r is the falling factorial. Call I realizable if all weights are nonnegative throughout some nondegenerate p-interval. Define Msp(n,k)=max_I(a_{k/2}+1−a₁) over realizable I.

**Source.** Berend, Ernst, Kontorovich and Kumar. *Exact Expressions for the Maximal Probability that all k-wise Independent Bits are 1*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10959-026-01487-4) Location: Conjecture 5.12, p. 41 of 45.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: May 2026 version of record inspected; no later resolution found in the bounded 9 October 2026 check.

**Further links.** [1](https://repository.rice.edu/items/dd3422cf-8733-4131-8155-edd1fad444c7)

## Q3792. Polynomial growth from nonnegative discrete curvature

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 38

If G has maximum degree D₀<∞ and satisfies CD(0,∞), must some D depending only on D₀ satisfy |{y:dist(x,y)&lt;r}|≤rᴰ for every x and integer r≥1?

**Context.** Origin: Blachar–Pajot–Salez, Edge-regular graphs with non-negative curvature have polynomial growth: Conjecture 1 restates the growth conjecture; Conjecture 2 asks a regular-graph refinement. Setup: Let G be connected, simple and locally finite. Set Δf(x)=Σ\_{y∼x}(f(y)−f(x)), Γ₀(f,g)=fg, and Γᵢ(f,g)=[ΔΓᵢ₋₁(f,g)−Γᵢ₋₁(f,Δg)−Γᵢ₋₁(g,Δf)]/2 for i=1,2. Write CD(κ,n) when Γ₂(f,f)≥κΓ₁(f,f)+(Δf)²/n pointwise for all f, interpreting 1/∞=0.

**Source.** Blachar, Pajot and Salez. *Edge-regular graphs with non-negative curvature have polynomial growth*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11094v1) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current June 2026 v1 inspected; no later version or resolution found by 9 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.unige.ch/~tranh/these-version-240521.pdf)

<a id="q3794"></a>

## Q3794. Cooperative-contact thresholds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

Do δc′(α)=δc(α) for all sufficiently small α≥0 and δc′(α)<δc(α) for all α<1 sufficiently close to 1?

**Context.** Origin: Latz–Swart, Monotone duality of interacting particle systems, §1.7. Setup: For α∈[0,1] and δ≥0, sites of ℤ² independently update: at rate 1−α choose a uniform neighbor and set xᵢ←max(xᵢ,xⱼ); at rate α choose two distinct uniform neighbors and set xᵢ←max(xᵢ,min(xⱼ,xₖ)); at rate δ reset to 0. Let ν̄ be the limit from all ones, ρ=ν̄(x₀=1), θ the survival probability from one occupied site, δc=sup{δ:ρ>0}, and δc′=sup{δ:θ>0}.

**Source.** Latz and Swart. *Monotone duality of interacting particle systems*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.00595v3) Location: §1.7, p. 11; Figure 1, p. 9.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current v3 (3 November 2025) inspected; Swart lists AIHP acceptance. No later final or resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://library.utia.cas.cz/separaty/2024/SI/latz-0598658.pdf)

<a id="q3797"></a>

## Q3797. Stability of randomly slowed shrinkers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 12) · **Collection** 38

For every d≥1, shrinker φ and r∈(0,1], is lim_{p↓0}ρ\_r(p)=1?

**Context.** Origin: Swart–Szabó–Toninelli, Peierls bounds from random Toom contours, Conjecture 12. Setup: For a nonconstant monotone local map φ:{0,1}^{ℤᵈ}→{0,1}, let A(φ) be its finite inclusion-minimal sets A satisfying φ(1_A)=1. Put εφ(ℓ)=max_{A∈A(φ)}min_{i∈A}ℓ(i). Call φ a shrinker if linear forms L₁,…,Lσ, σ≥2, sum to 0, all εφ(Ls)≥0, and Σεφ(Ls)>0. At each discrete space-time site independently, update by zero with probability p, by the shifted φ with probability (1−p)r, and otherwise retain its value. Let ρ\_r(p) be the limiting occupied-site density from all ones.

**Source.** Swart, Szabó and Toninelli. *Peierls bounds from random Toom contours*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.09660v1) Location: Conjecture 12, p. 12; definitions (1.9), (1.11), (1.25), (1.42).

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current arXiv v1 (16 January 2025) inspected. The May 2026 final Toom paper is the distinct earlier arXiv 2202.10999. No resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://library.utia.cas.cz/separaty/2024/SI/latz-0598658.pdf)


<a id="q3798"></a>

## Q3798. Irreducible Gaussian limits at arbitrary combinatorial dimensions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

For every integer d≥3 and α∈(1,d), do such Eₙ of combinatorial dimension α exist for which Zₙ is irreducible and converges in distribution to N(0,1)?

**Context.** Origin: Caravenna–Cottini–Peccati, On irreducible central limit theorems, Q2. Setup: Let Vₙ be finite sets with |Vₙ|→∞ and Eₙ⊂Vₙᵈ be permutation-invariant sets of distinct-coordinate tuples. They have combinatorial dimension α if constants c,C>0 satisfy |Eₙ|≥c|Vₙ|ᵅ and |Eₙ∩(A₁×⋯×A_d)|≤Cmaxᵢ|Aᵢ|ᵅ for all Aᵢ⊂Vₙ. For iid standard Gaussians Xᵥ put Zₙ=Σ\_{(v₁,…,v_d)∈Eₙ}∏ᵢXᵥᵢ/√(d!|Eₙ|). Call this sequence reducible if disjoint Bₙ,₁,…,Bₙ,mₙ⊂Vₙ exist with mₙ→∞, Σⱼ|Eₙ∩Bₙ,ⱼᵈ|/|Eₙ|→1 and maxⱼ|Eₙ∩Bₙ,ⱼᵈ|/|Eₙ|→0; otherwise irreducible.

**Source.** Caravenna, Cottini and Peccati. *On irreducible central limit theorems*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00748v2) Location: Q2, p. 14; Definitions 2.1, p. 7, and 2.8, p. 10.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Latest arXiv v2 (16 December 2025) and current author copy both retain Q2. No later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://orbilu.uni.lu/handle/10993/63749)

## Q3799. Sharp noise sensitivity of Boolean percolation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

For every t_L≥0 with t_LL²α₄(L)→∞, does Cov(f_L(η⁰),f_L(η^{t_L}))→0 as L→∞?

**Context.** Origin: Bhattacharjee–Peccati–Yogeshwaran, Spectra of Poisson functionals and applications in continuum percolation; unfinished Boolean-model extension after Theorem 1.4. Setup: Let η be planar Poisson points at the critical intensity λc for percolation of their closed unit disks. Evolve stationarily by births with intensity λc dxdt and independent rate 1 deaths. Let f_L be +1 if the disk union has a left-right crossing of W_L=[−L,L]², and −1 otherwise. Let α₄(L) be the probability of four disjoint alternating occupied/vacant arms from ∂W₁ to ∂W_L inside W_L∖W₁.

**Source.** Bhattacharjee, Peccati and Yogeshwaran. *Spectra of Poisson functionals and applications in continuum percolation*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13502v1) Location: §1.2, (1.1), p. 6; discussion after Theorem 1.4, pp. 8–9.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current arXiv v1 (18 July 2024) and current author preprint listing checked; only the weaker sharp instability is proved for this model. No later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://orbilu.uni.lu/handle/10993/63749)


<a id="q3800"></a>

## Q3800. Fast nonlinear exchange up to the marginal threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

For every δ∈(0,1), is there c_δ>0, independent of G and p, such that 0&lt;m_v≤(1−δ)/(Δ+1) for every v implies ||Tᵗ(p)−μ||\_TV≤n exp(−c_δ t/n) for all integers t≥0?

**Context.** Origin: Abbaszadeh Minab–Caputo–Chen–Morellini–Sinclair, Nonlinear Exchange Dynamics for Independent Sets, §6(2). Setup: Let G be a finite simple graph with n vertices and maximum degree Δ. For a law p on its independent sets, obtain T(p) by drawing two independent configurations from p, choosing a uniform vertex, exchanging their bits there if both outputs remain independent sets, and keeping the first output. Write m_v=p(v is occupied). Under the marginal bound below, let μ be the unique hard-core law μ(σ)∝∏\_{v∈σ}λ\_v with these marginals.

**Source.** Abbaszadeh Minab, Caputo, Chen, Morellini and Sinclair. *Nonlinear Exchange Dynamics for Independent Sets*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29016v1) Location: §6(2), p. 60; Theorem 4.3, p. 29; dynamics §2.5–2.6, pp. 11–13.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current July 2026 v1 inspected; no later version or resolution located on 9 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arcadia.sba.uniroma3.it/handle/2307/40879)
